'''Alibaba Group Holding Limited''', branded as '''Alibaba''' ({{IPAc-en|ˌ|æ|l|i|ˈ|b|ɑː|b|ə|,_|ˌ|ɑː|-}}), is a Chinese [[Multinational corporation|multinational]] [[technology company]] specializing in [[E-commerce in China|e-commerce]], retail, Internet, and technology. Founded on 28 June 1999<ref name="sec-form-f1" /> in [[Hangzhou]], Zhejiang, the company provides [[consumer-to-consumer]] (C2C), [[business-to-consumer]] (B2C), and [[business-to-business]] (B2B) sales services via Chinese and global marketplaces, as well as local consumer, digital media and entertainment, logistics, and [[cloud computing]] services. It owns and operates a diverse portfolio of companies around the world in numerous business sectors.

On 19 September 2014, Alibaba's American [[initial public offering]] (IPO) on the [[New York Stock Exchange]] raised US$25&nbsp;billion, giving the company a market value of US$231&nbsp;billion and, by far, then the [[Initial public offering#Largest IPOs by amount raised|largest IPO in world history]].<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Baker |first1=Lianna B. |last2=Toonkel |first2=Jessica |last3=Vlastelica |first3=Ryan |date=19 September 2014 |title=Alibaba surges 38 percent on massive demand in market debut |work=[[Reuters]] |editor-last=Orlofsky |editor-first=Steve |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-ipo/alibaba-surges-38-percent-on-massive-demand-in-market-debut-idUSKBN0HD2CO20140919 |url-status=live |access-date=12 October 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190213075131/https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-ipo/alibaba-surges-38-percent-on-massive-demand-in-market-debut-idUSKBN0HD2CO20140919 Alibaba is one of the world's [[list of largest Internet companies|largest retailers and e-commerce companies]]. == Naming ==
The company's name came from the character [[Ali Baba]] from the Middle Eastern folk-tale collection ''[[One Thousand and One Nights]]'' because of its universal appeal.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Grocer |first=Stephen |date=7 May 2014 |title=Alibaba's IPO Filing: Everything You Need to Know |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://blogs.wsj.com/digits/2014/05/06/alibabas-ipo-filing-everything-you-need-to-know/ |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=4 August 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171123051034/https://blogs.wsj.com/digits/2014/05/06/alibabas-ipo-filing-everything-you-need-to-know/ |archive-date=23 November 2017 }}</ref>

== History ==
===Founding===
On 28 June 1999,<ref name="sec-form-f1" /> [[Jack Ma]], with 17 friends and students founded Alibaba.com, a China-based [[Business-to-business|B2B]] marketplace site, in his [[Hangzhou]] apartment. In October 1999, Alibaba received a US$25&nbsp;million investment from [[Investor AB]],<ref name="Investors mäktiga miljardmiss i kinesiskt megabolag | SvD">{{Cite news |last=Andersson |first=Klas |title=Investors mäktiga miljardmiss i kinesiskt megabolag |url=https://www.svd.se/a/618fc4d6-23aa-3dfa-8c27-99566b2fca1e/investors-maktiga-miljardmiss-i-kinesiskt-megabolag |website=Svenska Dagbladet |date=9 May 2014 |archive-date=7 February 2023 |access-date=7 February 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230207024621/https://www.svd.se/a/618fc4d6-23aa-3dfa-8c27-99566b2fca1e/investors-maktiga-miljardmiss-i-kinesiskt-megabolag |url-status=live }}</ref> [[Goldman Sachs]] and [[SoftBank Group|SoftBank]]. In 1999, Investor AB owned 6% of the shares.<ref name="Investors mäktiga miljardmiss i kinesiskt megabolag | SvD" /> In 2002, Alibaba.com became profitable.<ref name=":2"/> 

===Taobao formation===
When [[eBay]] announced its expansion into China in 2003, Ma viewed the American company as a foreign competitor and created Taobao as a subsidiary to try and win the domestic market. Taobao gained consumer trust in the Chinese e-commerce market with features like third party verification and offering most services for free before making a return on additional services.<ref name=":2">{{Cite news |last=Popovic |first=Stevan |date=4 May 2014 |title=Jack Ma: The man leading the Chinese e-commerce market |work=Hot Topics |url=http://www.hottopics.ht/stories/consumer/jack-ma-the-man-leading-the-chinese-e-commerce-market/ |url-status=live |access-date=17 January 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160112115009/https://www.hottopics.ht/stories/consumer/jack-ma-the-man-leading-the-chinese-e-commerce-market/ |archive-date=12 January 2016 }}</ref> Taobao surpassed eBay in 2007,<ref name=":2"/> and eBay later closed its unprofitable China Web unit.<ref name="autogenerated1">{{Cite web |date=2 March 2017 |title=The Rags-to-Riches Life Story of Alibaba Founder Jack Ma |url=https://www.inc.com/business-insider/alibaba-jack-ma-life-story.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170303091205/https://www.inc.com/business-insider/alibaba-jack-ma-life-story.html |archive-date=3 March 2017 |access-date=17 January 2019 |website=Inc.com }}</ref><ref name=":3">{{Cite web |last=Chen |first=Lilu Yilun |date=19 November 2017 |title=Quicktake - Alibaba |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/quicktake/alibaba |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190117232527/https://www.bloomberg.com/quicktake/alibaba |archive-date=17 January 2019 |access-date=17 January 2019 |website=www.bloomberg.com }}</ref>

===Pre-IPO expansion===
In 2005, [[Yahoo!]] invested in Alibaba through a [[variable interest entity]] (VIE) structure, buying a 40% stake in the company for US$1&nbsp;billion.<ref name=":2" /><ref name=":3" /> In 2012, [[China Investment Corporation]] led a group of Chinese investors in buying out Yahoo!'s 40% stake and in buying the Alibaba shares that had traded on the [[Hong Kong Stock Exchange]].<ref name=":32">{{Cite book |last=Liu |first=Zongyuan Zoe |title=Sovereign Funds: How the Communist Party of China Finances its Global Ambitions |publisher=The Belknap Press of [[Harvard University Press]] |year=2023 |isbn=9780674271913 |pages= |doi=10.2307/jj.2915805 |jstor=jj.2915805 |s2cid=259402050 }}</ref>{{Rp|page=130}}

According to Li Chuan, a senior executive at Alibaba, the company was planning in 2013 to open traditional brick and mortar retail outlets in partnership with Wanda Group, a Chinese real estate company.<ref name="hsu2013">{{Cite web |last=Hsu |first=Alex |date=3 July 2013 |title=Alibaba to Open Brick and Mortar Stores in Wanda Plazas Across China |url=http://bw-original-reporting.tumblr.com/post/54526405940/alibaba-to-open-brick-and-mortar-stores-in-wanda-plazas |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131013150400/http://bw-original-reporting.tumblr.com/post/54526405940/alibaba-to-open-brick-and-mortar-stores-in-wanda-plazas |archive-date=13 October 2013 |website=BrightWire News }}</ref> Additionally, Alibaba purchased a 25% stake in Hong Kong-listed Chinese department store chain [[Intime Department Store|Intime Retail]] in early 2014.<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Mozur |first1=Paul |last2=Fung |first2=Esther |date=31 March 2014 |title=Alibaba to Pay $692 Million for Stake in Intime Retail |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-to-pay-692-million-for-35-stake-in-intime-retail-1396234141 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200115200014/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-to-pay-692-million-for-35-stake-in-intime-retail-1396234141 |archive-date=15 January 2020 }}</ref> In early 2017, Alibaba and Intime's founder Shen Guojun agreed to pay HK$19.8&nbsp;billion (US$2.6&nbsp;billion) to take the store chain private. Alibaba's stake—28% from 2014's US$692&nbsp;million investment—rose about 74% after the deal.<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Lin |first1=Liza |last2=Becker |first2=Nathan |date=10 January 2017 |title=Alibaba Bags Chinese Retailer Intime in $2.6 Billion Deal |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-seeks-to-take-intime-private-with-founder-1484005494 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=5 March 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180910070605/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-seeks-to-take-intime-private-with-founder-1484005494 |archive-date=10 September 2018 }}</ref>

In April 2014, Alibaba invested in Lyft, along with [[Coatue Management]], and [[Andreessen Horowitz]]; they led a US$250&nbsp;million Series D financing round.<ref name=":3" /><ref>{{Cite web |title=Lyft Raises $250 Million From Coatue, Alibaba, And Third Point To Expand Internationally |url=https://techcrunch.com/2014/04/02/lyft-250m/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190217090443/https://techcrunch.com/2014/04/02/lyft-250m/ |archive-date=17 February 2019 |access-date=17 January 2019 |website=TechCrunch |date=2 April 2014 |language=en-US }}</ref> On 5 June 2014, Alibaba bought a 50% stake of [[Guangzhou Evergrande Taobao F.C.|Guangzhou Evergrande F.C.]] from [[Evergrande Real Estate|Evergrande Real Estate Group Ltd.]] in a deal that was worth 1.2&nbsp;billion yuan (US$192&nbsp;million).<ref name=":3" /><ref>{{Cite web |date=5 June 2014 |title=Alibaba Buys Into Soccer Team After Billionaires' Drinks |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2014-06-05/evergrande-rises-on-alibaba-soccer-stake-report-hong-kong-mover |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190117231019/https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2014-06-05/evergrande-rises-on-alibaba-soccer-stake-report-hong-kong-mover |archive-date=17 January 2019 |access-date=17 January 2019 |website=www.bloomberg.com }}</ref> 

===IPO===
On 5 September 2014, Alibaba set a [[United States dollar|US$]]60- to $66- per-share price range for its scheduled [[initial public offering]] (IPO). On 18 September 2014, Alibaba's IPO priced at US$68, raising US$25&nbsp;billion for the company and investors.<ref name="Bloomberg"/><ref name="w68"/> Alibaba was the biggest IPO in history, bigger than Google, Facebook, and Twitter combined.<ref>{{Cite journal |last=Zhang |first=Yuejiao |year=2017 |title=Assessing Attitudes Toward Content and Design in Alibaba's Dry Goods Business Infographics |journal=Journal of Business and Technical Communication |language=en |volume=31 |issue=1 |pages=30–62 |doi=10.1177/1050651916667530 |s2cid=64309912 |doi-access=free }}</ref><ref name="w68">{{Cite news |last1=Demos |first1=Telis |last2=Jarzemsky |first2=Matt |date=18 September 2014 |title=Alibaba's IPO Priced at $68 a Share |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibabas-ipo-priced-at-68-a-share-1411075675 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141026212525/http://online.wsj.com/articles/alibabas-ipo-priced-at-68-a-share-1411075675 |archive-date=26 October 2014 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |date=18 September 2014 |title=Alibaba prices at $68, becomes top U.S. IPO |work=USA Today |url=https://www.usatoday.com/story/money/markets/2014/09/18/alibaba-deal-price-ipo/15846185/ |url-status=live |access-date=18 September 2014 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190115190357/https://www.usatoday.com/story/money/markets/2014/09/18/alibaba-deal-price-ipo/15846185/ |archive-date=15 January 2019 }}</ref> On 22 September 2014, Alibaba's underwriters announced their confirmation that they had exercised a [[greenshoe option]] to sell 15% more shares than originally planned.<ref name="Bloomberg">{{Cite news |last1=Picker |first1=Leslie |last2=Chen |first2=Lulu Yilun |date=22 September 2014 |title=Alibaba's Banks Boost IPO Size to Record of $25 Billion |work=Bloomberg L.P. |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/2014-09-22/alibaba-s-banks-said-to-increase-ipo-size-to-record-25-billion.html |url-status=live |access-date=23 September 2014 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150115083410/http://www.bloomberg.com/news/2014-09-22/alibaba-s-banks-said-to-increase-ipo-size-to-record-25-billion.html |archive-date=15 January 2015 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Barreto |first=Elzio |date=21 September 2014 |title=Alibaba IPO ranks as world's biggest after additional shares sold |work=[[Reuters]] |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-ipo-value-idUSKCN0HH0A620140922 |url-status=live |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200607160132/https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-ipo-value-idUSKCN0HH0A620140922 |archive-date=7 June 2020 }}</ref>

Alibaba and the underwriters of its IPO were sued in a California superior court in a consolidated [[class action]] lawsuit.<ref name="climbed">{{Cite news |title=Alibaba Enters into a $75 Million Settlement Deal; Shares Climbed 2% in Pre-market |work=capitalwatch.com |url=https://www.capitalwatch.com/article-3272-1.html |url-status=live |access-date=5 February 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190207014911/https://www.capitalwatch.com/article-3272-1.html |archive-date=7 February 2019 }}</ref> The lawsuit was filed in October 2015 on behalf of investors who purchased Alibaba's [[American depositary shares]] alleging violations of the [[Securities Act]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Schacknow |first=Peter |date=31 December 2018 |title=Stocks making the biggest moves premarket: AMZN, DB, VZ, GOOGL & more |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2018/12/31/stocks-making-the-biggest-moves-premarket-amzn-db-vz-googl--more.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190207020302/https://www.cnbc.com/2018/12/31/stocks-making-the-biggest-moves-premarket-amzn-db-vz-googl--more.html |archive-date=7 February 2019 |access-date=5 February 2019 |website=CNBC }}</ref> Alibaba reached a settlement agreement in December 2018, subject to court approval, in which it agreed to pay $75&nbsp;million to settle the lawsuit.<ref name=climbed/><ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba Group Enters into Settlement of Securities Act Class Action Initiated in October 2015 |url=https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1577552/000110465918075203/a18-42169_1ex99d1.htm |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200725025318/https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1577552/000110465918075203/a18-42169_1ex99d1.htm |archive-date=25 July 2020 |access-date=5 February 2019 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=11 January 2019 |title=Alibaba settlement |url=https://images.law.com/contrib/content/uploads/documents/407/1682/Alibaba-settlement.pdf |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190207014946/https://images.law.com/contrib/content/uploads/documents/407/1682/Alibaba-settlement.pdf |archive-date=7 February 2019 |access-date=5 February 2019 }}</ref>

===Minor acquisitions===
In 2015, Alibaba's Mobile Business Group formed Ali Literature to enter the [[Chinese online literature|internet literature sector]].<ref name=":02">{{Cite book |last=Han|first=Rongbin|title=Make China Great Again: Online Alt-History Fiction and Popular Authoritarianism|date=2026|publisher=[[Columbia University Press]]|isbn=978-0-231-22054-5|location=New York}}</ref>{{Reference page|page=26}}

Beginning in mid-2015, Alibaba invested in [[Paytm]], an India-based mobile payment platform.<ref>{{Cite book |title=China's Digital Expansion in the Global South |date=2024 |publisher=[[Routledge]] |isbn=978-1-032-94328-2 |editor-last=Foster |editor-first=Christopher |location=London |chapter=China's Digital Expansion in the Global South: Systemic Literature Review and Future Research Agenda |editor-last2=Gao |editor-first2=Ping |editor-last3=Han |editor-first3=Xia |editor-last4=Heeks |editor-first4=Richard |editor-last5=Jepson |editor-first5=Nicholas |editor-last6=Schindler |editor-first6=Seth |editor-last7=Zhou |editor-first7=Qingna}}</ref>{{Rp|page=9}}

Alibaba is among the most prominent investors in Thailand's [[Eastern Economic Corridor]], which was opened in 2018.<ref name=":Han">{{Cite book |last=Han |first=Enze |title=The Ripple Effect: China's Complex Presence in Southeast Asia |date=2024 |publisher=[[Oxford University Press]] |isbn=978-0-19-769659-0 |location=New York, NY }}</ref>{{Rp|page=57}} Alibaba committed approximately US$320 million for the development of an e-commerce digital hub in the EEC.<ref name=":Han" />{{Rp|page=57}}

In January 2017, Alibaba and the [[International Olympic Committee]] jointly announced an $800&nbsp;million deal that would last until 2028 where the company would sponsor the [[Olympic Games]].<ref name=":3" /><ref>{{Cite web |date=19 January 2017 |title=Alibaba Olympics Sponsorship Deal Said to Be Worth $800 Million |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2017-01-19/alibaba-will-put-its-technology-to-the-test-on-an-olympian-stage |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190117232530/https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2017-01-19/alibaba-will-put-its-technology-to-the-test-on-an-olympian-stage |archive-date=17 January 2019 |access-date=17 January 2019 |website=www.bloomberg.com }}</ref>

===Leadership change===
[[File:20th Anniversary Schwab Foundation Gala Dinner (44887783681) (cropped).jpg|thumb|[[Jack Ma]], founder and the former executive chairman of the Alibaba Group]]
In September 2018, Jack Ma, the main founder of Alibaba, announced that he would step down as chairman in a year's time so he could focus on philanthropy.<ref>{{Cite news |title=China will struggle to produce another Jack Ma |language=en |newspaper=The Economist |url=https://www.economist.com/leaders/2018/09/15/china-will-struggle-to-produce-another-jack-ma |url-status=live |access-date=17 September 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181225092253/https://www.economist.com/leaders/2018/09/15/china-will-struggle-to-produce-another-jack-ma |archive-date=25 December 2018 }}</ref> In response to the announcement, ''[[The Economist]]'' stated that Ma had a significant impact in China and worldwide.<ref name=":0">{{Cite news |title=China will struggle to produce another Jack Ma |language=en |newspaper=The Economist |url=https://www.economist.com/leaders/2018/09/15/china-will-struggle-to-produce-another-jack-ma |url-status=live |access-date=19 September 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181225092253/https://www.economist.com/leaders/2018/09/15/china-will-struggle-to-produce-another-jack-ma |archive-date=25 December 2018 }}</ref> On Tuesday, 10 September 2019, Jack Ma officially stepped down as the chairman of Alibaba, and [[Daniel Zhang]] succeeded him at the head of the company.{{cn|date=April 2026}}

===Hong Kong listing===
In November 2019, Alibaba raised 12.9&nbsp;billion in its secondary listing in Hong Kong, which became the world's largest offering that year.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2019/11/26/alibaba-shares-jump-more-than-6percent-in-hong-kong-debut.html |title=Alibaba shares surge in Hong Kong debut, world's largest listing so far in 2019 |publisher=CNBC |date=25 November 2019 }}</ref> 

In November 2020, [[Paramount leader|Chinese leader]] [[Xi Jinping]] blocked the [[Ant Group]] IPO.<ref name="Xi Jinping's decision">{{Cite web |last1=Yang |first1=Jing |last2=Wei |first2=Lingling |date=12 November 2020 |title=China's President Xi Jinping Personally Scuttled Jack Ma's Ant IPO |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/china-president-xi-jinping-halted-jack-ma-ant-ipo-11605203556 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201113061215/https://www.wsj.com/articles/china-president-xi-jinping-halted-jack-ma-ant-ipo-11605203556 |archive-date=13 November 2020 |access-date=24 December 2020 |website=The Wall Street Journal }}</ref> Economist Bo Zhuang stated the block was part of the CCP's increased scrutiny of finance and technology companies.<ref name=":5">{{Cite web |title=Jack Ma vs. the Party: Inside the collapse of the world's biggest IPO |url=https://asia.nikkei.com/Spotlight/Most-read-in-2020/Jack-Ma-vs.-the-Party-Inside-the-collapse-of-the-world-s-biggest-IPO |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210104063209/https://asia.nikkei.com/Spotlight/Most-read-in-2020/Jack-Ma-vs.-the-Party-Inside-the-collapse-of-the-world-s-biggest-IPO |archive-date=4 January 2021 |access-date=3 January 2021 |website=Nikkei Asia |language=en-GB }}</ref>

In December 2020, the shares of Alibaba Group suffered a stock price crash to the lowest close in around 6 months, following the antitrust investigation into the company by Chinese regulators.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba stock suffers record fall after antitrust probe in China |url=https://www.marketwatch.com/story/alibaba-stock-suffering-record-drop-after-antitrust-probe-in-china-2020-12-24 |url-status=live |access-date=24 December 2020 |website=MarketWatch |date=24 December 2020 }}</ref> In December 2020, China's [[State Administration for Market Regulation]] (SAMR) stated that it opened an investigation into Alibaba over monopolistic practices.<ref name="cnbc_Alib">{{Cite web |last=Cheng |first=Evelyn |date=23 December 2020 |title=Alibaba shares fall after reports of anti-monopoly probe 2020 |title=China Tells Ant Group to Refocus on Its Payments Business |language=en-US |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] ''[[People's Daily]]'', the official newspaper of the [[Central Committee of the Chinese Communist Party]], endorsed the investigation shortly after the announcement, claiming the investigation to be "an important step in strengthening antimonopoly oversight in the internet sphere".<ref>{{Cite web |title=人民日报：加强反垄断监管是为了更好发展 |url=https://wap.peopleapp.com/article/6082652/5994036 |url-status=live from the antitrust probe, Alibaba lost nearly all of its stock-market gains in 2020, from $859&nbsp;billion to $586&nbsp;billion, by the end of December.<ref>{{Cite news |author1=Xie Yu |author2=Chong December 2020 |issn=0099-9660 }}</ref> Jack Ma, co-founder of both Alibaba and [[Ant Group]], vanished from public view when Ant's IPO was suspended in early November,<ref name="bloo_Chin">{{Cite news |last1=Chen |first1=Lulu Yilun |last2=Liu |first2=Coco |date=23 December 2020 |title=China Targets Jack Ma's Alibaba Empire in Monopoly Probe |work=[[Bloomberg News]] |agency=[[Bloomberg News]] }}</ref> but resurfaced in January 2021 in a 50-second video, appearing briefly via video link at the digitally facilitated Rural Teacher Initiative. As of February 2021, he has yet to be seen in public.<ref>{{Cite web |date=20 January 2021 |title=Jack Ma gives video speech to rural teachers after three months of silence |url=https://www.scmp.com/tech/big-tech/article/3118454/alibaba-founder-jack-ma-delivers-video-speech-chinas-rural-teachers |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210204035213/https://www.scmp.com/tech/big-tech/article/3118454/alibaba-founder-jack-ma-delivers-video-speech-chinas-rural-teachers |archive-date=4 February 2021 |access-date=3 February 2021 |website=South China Morning Post |language=en }}</ref> The video appearance caused Alibaba stock to increase 7%.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba stock spikes after Jack Ma resurfaces |url=https://abcnews.go.com/Business/alibaba-stock-spikes-jack-ma-resurfaces/story?id=75401864 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210204123102/https://abcnews.go.com/Business/alibaba-stock-spikes-jack-ma-resurfaces/story?id=75401864 |archive-date=4 February 2021 |access-date=3 February 2021 |website=ABC News |language=en }}</ref> Jack Ma made a public appearance in Hangzhou, China in March 2023 where he met with students and teachers at the Alibaba partners-funded Yungu School.<ref>[https://www.scmp.com/business/china-business/article/3217848/jack-ma-takes-university-hong-kongs-honorary-professorship-pursuit-his-post-retirement-passion][[South China Morning Post]]</ref>

In February 2021, Alibaba sold $5&nbsp;billion in bonds, the company's third large sale of [[Bond (finance)|dollar bonds]], issuing four sets priced to yield between 2.143% and 3.251%. The four sets of bonds were $1.5&nbsp;billion of both 10-year and 30-year debt along with $1&nbsp;billion of bonds due in 20 and 40 years. The 20-year bonds were designated as [[Economic dimensions of sustainability|sustainability notes]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Webb |first=Quentin |date=4 February 2021 |title=Alibaba Plans Up to $5 Billion Bond Sale |language=en-US |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-plans-up-to-5-billion-bond-sale-11612330820 |url-status=live |access-date=5 February 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210206015228/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-plans-up-to-5-billion-bond-sale-11612330820 |archive-date=6 February 2021 |issn=0099-9660 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Yoon |first=Frances |date=5 February 2021 |title=Alibaba Sells $5 Billion of Bonds |language=en-US |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-sells-5-billion-of-bonds-11612512624 |url-status=live |access-date=5 February 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210205124011/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-sells-5-billion-of-bonds-11612512624 |archive-date=5 February 2021 |issn=0099-9660 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |author1=Julie Wernau |author2=Manju Dalal |date=29 November 2017 |title=Alibaba Sells $7 Billion in Dollar Bonds |language=en-US |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-flies-back-into-u-s-dollar-bond-markets-1511957725 |url-status=live |access-date=5 February 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210206015327/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-flies-back-into-u-s-dollar-bond-markets-1511957725 |archive-date=6 February 2021 |issn=0099-9660 }}</ref>

On 9 April 2021, as part of a Chinese [[2020–2021 Xi Jinping Administration reform spree|crackdown on big tech]], SAMR issued a $2.8&nbsp;billion fine against Alibaba for anti-competitive practices and ordered Alibaba to file self-examination and compliance reports to the SAMR for three years. This amounted to 12% {{Webarchive |url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210603150608/https://potstickers.ai/invest/alibaba-nasdaq-baba-fined-2-75-billion-now-what/ |date=3 June 2021 }} of its 2020 net profit. Critics say the move tightens the Chinese's governments control of tech companies.<ref>{{Cite web |date=10 April 2021 |title=Alibaba fined $2.8 billion on competition charge in China |url=https://apnews.com/article/business-china-6639cb02a43f5e2b789313177dcd7542 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210410035925/https://apnews.com/article/business-china-6639cb02a43f5e2b789313177dcd7542 |archive-date=10 April 2021 |access-date=10 April 2021 |website=AP NEWS }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Wang |first=Christine |date=10 April 2021 |title=China slaps Alibaba with $2.8 billion fine in anti-monopoly probe |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2021/04/09/china-fines-alibaba-in-anti-monopoly-probe.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210410035925/https://www.cnbc.com/2021/04/09/china-fines-alibaba-in-anti-monopoly-probe.html |archive-date=10 April 2021 |access-date=10 April 2021 |website=CNBC |language=en }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |agency=Agence France-Presse |date=10 April 2021 |title=China fines Alibaba billions for alleged market abuses |url=http://www.theguardian.com/business/2021/apr/10/china-fines-alibaba-billions-for-alleged-market-abuses |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210410035208/https://www.theguardian.com/business/2021/apr/10/china-fines-alibaba-billions-for-alleged-market-abuses |archive-date=10 April 2021 |access-date=10 April 2021 |website=the Guardian |language=en }}</ref> Alibaba stated that it would dedicate itself to correct its errors and accepted the fine without making any appeal.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=57}}

On 11 November 2021, it was reported that over the course of its [[Singles' Day|11-day Singles' Day]] sales extravaganza, Chinese e-commerce giant Alibaba Group Holding received 540.3&nbsp;billion yuan (S$114.4&nbsp;billion) in orders, a 14 percent increase over the previous year.<ref>{{Cite news |date=12 November 2021 |title=Alibaba takes in record $114.4 billion in Singles' Day sales |language=en |work=The Straits Times |url=https://www.straitstimes.com/asia/east-asia/alibaba-takes-in-record-1144-billion-in-singles-day-sales |url-status=live |access-date=12 November 2021 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211112004834/https://www.straitstimes.com/asia/east-asia/alibaba-takes-in-record-1144-billion-in-singles-day-sales |archive-date=12 November 2021 |issn=0585-3923 }}</ref>

In July 2022, the SEC added Alibaba to a list of companies facing delisting from U.S. stock exchanges if its auditors remain unable to examine Alibaba's books before 2024.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Chan |first=Michelle |date=29 July 2022 |title=SEC Adds Alibaba to List of Chinese Companies Facing Delisting |language=en-US |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/sec-adds-alibaba-to-list-of-chinese-companies-facing-delisting-11659130698 |access-date=30 July 2022 |issn=0099-9660 |archive-date=30 July 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220730004306/https://www.wsj.com/articles/sec-adds-alibaba-to-list-of-chinese-companies-facing-delisting-11659130698 |url-status=live }}</ref>

In December 2022, a [[State-owned enterprises of China|state-owned enterprise]] of the [[China Internet Investment Fund]], established by the [[Cyberspace Administration of China]], took a 1 percent [[golden share]] investment in two Alibaba subsidiaries that control [[Youku]] and [[UCWeb]].<ref>{{Cite news |date=13 January 2023 |title=China acquires 'golden shares' in two Alibaba units |language=en |work=[[Reuters]] |url=https://www.reuters.com/technology/china-moving-take-golden-shares-alibaba-tencent-units-ft-2023-01-13/ |access-date=15 January 2023 |archive-date=11 May 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230511120113/https://www.reuters.com/technology/china-moving-take-golden-shares-alibaba-tencent-units-ft-2023-01-13/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

===2023 restructuring to present===
In March 2023, Alibaba announced its "1+6+N" restructuring plan, which reorganized its business structure into six independently run entities: Cloud Intelligence Group, Taobao and Tmall Group, Cainiao Smart Logistics Network, Local Services group, Alibaba International Digital Commerce, and the Digital Media and Entertainment group.<ref>{{cite news |title=Alibaba to overhaul China's biggest tech conglomerate into 6 units to reignite their entrepreneurial mojo as 'start-ups' |url=https://finance.yahoo.com/news/alibaba-overhaul-chinas-biggest-tech-093000159.html |access-date=25 November 2023 |work=Yahoo Finance |publisher=South China Morning Post |date=28 March 2023 |archive-date=25 November 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231125202805/https://finance.yahoo.com/news/alibaba-overhaul-chinas-biggest-tech-093000159.html |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last1=Huang |first1=Raffaele |last2=Leong |first2=Clarence |date=28 March 2023 |title=Alibaba to Split Into Six Groups and Explore IPOs in a Departure From Jack Ma Era |work=The Wall Street Journal |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-to-split-into-six-separate-groups-in-biggest-shake-up-9ce2201f?mod=hp_lead_pos1 |access-date=28 March 2023 |archive-date=28 March 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230328111732/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-to-split-into-six-separate-groups-in-biggest-shake-up-9ce2201f?mod=hp_lead_pos1 |url-status=live }}</ref> Each business unit would have their own CEO and board of directors and be able to seek their own fundraising and market listings.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Yordan |title=Alibaba to restructure as six separate companies |url=https://www.gsmarena.com/alibaba_is_facing_restructuring_to_keep_businesses_running-news-58050.php |access-date=29 March 2023 |website=GSMArena.com |language=en-US |archive-date=29 March 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230329062638/https://www.gsmarena.com/alibaba_is_facing_restructuring_to_keep_businesses_running-news-58050.php |url-status=live }}</ref> Under the new "1+6+N" structure, Alibaba also established a Capital Management Committee and a Compliance and Risk Committee.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Yang |first1=Ivy |title=Can Alibaba's six-ring circus succeed? |url=https://thechinaproject.com/2023/06/01/can-alibabas-six-ring-circus-succeed/ |website=The China Project |access-date=25 November 2023 |date=1 June 2023 |archive-date=25 November 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231125202803/https://thechinaproject.com/2023/06/01/can-alibabas-six-ring-circus-succeed/ |url-status=live }}</ref> ''[[The Wall Street Journal]]'' reported on 30 March that Jack Ma engineered this in talks with company CEO [[Daniel Zhang]] while he was overseas.<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Yang |first1=Jing |last2=Lu |first2=Shen |date=30 March 2023 |title=Jack Ma Engineered Alibaba's Breakup From Overseas |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/jack-ma-engineered-alibabas-breakup-from-overseas-91f2d574?mod=hp_lead_pos2 |access-date=30 March 2023 }}</ref>

In September 2023, Cainiao Smart Logistics Network Ltd., the logistics arm of Alibaba Group, has filed for its Hong Kong initial public offering, potentially making it among the first of the Chinese e-commerce leader's units to go public.<ref>{{Cite news |date=26 September 2023 |title=Alibaba's Logistics Arm Files for $1 Billion-Plus IPO |language=en |work=Bloomberg.com |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2023-09-26/alibaba-to-spin-off-cainiao-logistics-unit-for-hong-kong-ipo |access-date=27 September 2023 |archive-date=10 January 2024 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240110205925/https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2023-09-26/alibaba-to-spin-off-cainiao-logistics-unit-for-hong-kong-ipo |url-status=live }}</ref>

In December 2024, Alibaba announced it would sell [[Intime Department Store]] to [[Youngor Group]].<ref>{{Cite web |date=16 December 2024 |title=Alibaba to sell department store chain Intime for $1 bln |url=https://www.reuters.com/markets/deals/alibaba-sell-mall-chain-1-bln-part-latest-overhaul-2024-12-17/ |website=Reuters }}</ref>

In November 2025, the ''[[Financial Times]]'' published a report stating that a U.S. national security memo accused Alibaba of providing capabilities to the [[People's Liberation Army]] used to target the U.S.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Sevastopulo |first=Demetri |date=14 November 2025 |title=White House memo claims Alibaba is helping Chinese military target US |url=https://www.ft.com/content/30fb83a0-8cb9-4805-b5d2-19b5ef510043 |url-access=subscription |access-date=16 November 2025 |work=[[Financial Times]]}}</ref> Alibaba disputed the report.<ref>{{Cite news |date=14 November 2025 |title=White House says Alibaba is helping Chinese military target US, FT reports |url=https://www.reuters.com/world/china/white-house-says-alibaba-is-helping-chinese-military-target-us-ft-reports-2025-11-14/ |access-date=16 November 2025 |work=[[Reuters]]}}</ref> In January 2026, Texas governor [[Greg Abbott]] prohibited Alibaba products and services on all government devices and networks.<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Rozen|first1=Courtney|last2=Alper|first2=Alexandra|last3=Sherpardson|first3=David|date=26 January 2026|title=Texas governor bars state employees from using Shein, Alibaba products|url=https://www.reuters.com/technology/texas-governor-bars-state-employees-using-alibaba-temu-products-2026-01-26/|access-date=26 January 2026|work=[[Reuters]]}}</ref> In June 2026, the U.S. added the company to its list of "Chinese military companies," citing its affiliation with the [[Ministry of Industry and Information Technology|PRC Ministry of Industry and Information Technology]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Power |first=John |title=US lists China's BYD, Alibaba, Baidu as 'Chinese military companies' |url=https://www.aljazeera.com/economy/2026/6/9/us-lists-chinas-byd-alibaba-baidu-as-chinese-military-companies |access-date=2026-06-09 |website=Al Jazeera |language=en}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Pentagon restores Alibaba, Baidu and BYD to Chinese military groups blacklist |url=https://www.ft.com/content/0689d87b-8ad3-4b7c-87e3-e06f07891a17?syn-25a6b1a6=1 |website=Financial Times}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=2026-06-08 |title=Entities Identified as Chinese Military Companies Operating in the United States in Accordance with Section 1260H of the William M. (Mac) Thornberry National Defense Authorization Act for Fiscal Year 2021 |url=https://media.defense.gov/2026/Jun/08/2003945537/-1/-1/1/ENTITIES-IDENTIFIED-AS-CHINESE-MILITARY-COMPANIES-OPERATING-IN-THE-UNITED-STATES-IN-ACCORDANCE-WITH-SECTION-1260H.PDF |access-date=2026-08-02 |website=[[U.S. Department of War]]}}</ref> Alibaba subsequently filed a lawsuit against the U.S. government in an attempt to be removed from the list.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Stempel |first=Jonathan |last2=Chiacu |first2=Doina |date=23 June 2026 |title=Alibaba sues US for being linked to Chinese military |url=https://www.reuters.com/legal/litigation/alibaba-sues-us-department-defense-branding-it-chinese-military-company-2026-06-23/ |access-date=24 June 2026 |work=[[Reuters]]}}</ref>
