Alibaba is viewed by the Chinese government as one of its [[National champions|national champion]] corporations.<ref name=":Curtis&Klaus">{{Cite book |last1=Curtis |first1=Simon |title=The Belt and Road City: Geopolitics, Urbanization, and China's Search for a New International Order |last2=Klaus |first2=Ian |publisher=[[Yale University Press]] |isbn=9780300266900 |location=New Haven and London |publication-date=2024 |doi=10.2307/jj.11589102 |jstor=jj.11589102 }}</ref>{{Rp|page=156}} Alibaba supplies a significant amount of technology for the [[Belt and Road Initiative|Digital Silk Road]].<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=272}}

==Companies and affiliated entities==
=== E-commerce and retail service platforms ===
[[File:Alibaba group Headquarters.jpg|thumb|Alibaba group headquarters]]
[[File:Alibaba Binjiang Park.jpg|thumb|240px|Alibaba [[Binjiang District|Binjiang]] Campus in Hangzhou, headquarters for Alibaba's B2B service<ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba Subsidiary Aboub B2B Marketplace |url=https://www.aboub.com/ |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171111100025/https://www.aboub.com/ |archive-date=11 November 2017 |access-date=11 November 2017 |website=ABOUB }}</ref>|alt=]] Buyers who visit Alibaba.com are looking to purchase wholesale from thousands of product categories.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2025-11-14 |title=Alibaba's wholesale platform empowers merchants with AI tool to source products |url=https://www.scmp.com/tech/big-tech/article/3332690/alibabas-wholesale-platform-empowers-merchants-new-ai-search-tool-source-products |access-date=2026-01-09 |website=South China Morning Post |language=en}}</ref> Alibaba.com later became the world's largest online B2B trading platform for small businesses as of 2014.<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Osawa |first1=Juro |last2=Mozurand |first2=Paul |last3=Winkler |first3=Rolfe |date=15 April 2014 |title=Alibaba Flexes Muscles Before IPO |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-flexes-muscles-ahead-of-ipo-1397605974 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200304134107/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-flexes-muscles-ahead-of-ipo-1397605974 |archive-date=4 March 2020 }}</ref> According to Alibaba.com's own website, there are 150,000 verified sellers located in more than 200 countries around the world.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Global Buying and Selling - Alibaba Global Trade Platform |url=https://seller.alibaba.com/solutions/global-buy-global-sell |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260225020356/https://seller.alibaba.com/solutions/global-buy-global-sell |archive-date=25 February 2026 |access-date=2026-05-06 |website=seller.alibaba.com |language=en}}</ref> It also indicates that there are more than 10 million buyers around the world. In 2019, Alibaba.com allowed small and medium-sized U.S. businesses to sell on the site, and the company was actively looking to tap into the B2B e-commerce market in the US. The opportunity provided US business-to-business companies with the ability to also sell to other US retailers and smaller commercial outfits. In 2019, one-third of buyers on the site were US-based, while more than 95% of sellers were from China.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba welcomes U.S. small businesses to sell globally on its platform |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/technology/alibaba-welcomes-us-small-businesses-to-sell-globally-on-its-platform-idUSKCN1UI1DU/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260113070806/https://www.reuters.com/article/technology/alibaba-welcomes-us-small-businesses-to-sell-globally-on-its-platform-idUSKCN1UI1DU/ |archive-date=13 January 2026 |website=Reuters}}</ref> Alibaba.com has three main services: the English language portal Alibaba.com, which handles sales between importers and exporters from nearly all countries,<ref name="home">{{Cite web |title=Alibaba Group |url=https://www.alibabagroup.com/en/global/home |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140504051836/http://news.alibaba.com/specials/aboutalibaba/aligroup/index.html |archive-date=4 May 2014 |access-date=19 January 2020 |website=Alibaba Group Group }}</ref> the Chinese portal 1688.com, which originally manages domestic B2B trade in China, and [[AliExpress]].com, a global consumer B2C marketplace.<ref>[https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1577552/000119312514184994/d709111df1.htm Alibaba Group Holding Limited] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210104065451/https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1577552/000119312514184994/d709111df1.htm |date=4 January 2021 }} ''[[U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission]]''</ref> In 2025, 1688.com has started marketing its platform globally. Alibaba.com went public at the Hong Kong Stock Exchange in 2007, and was delisted again in 2012.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba Group Said to Plan Privatizing Alibaba.com Close to IPO Price |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/2012-02-17/alibaba-said-to-plan-to-take-alibaba-com-private-for-close-to-ipo-price.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150110042623/http://www.bloomberg.com/news/2012-02-17/alibaba-said-to-plan-to-take-alibaba-com-private-for-close-to-ipo-price.html |archive-date=10 January 2015 |access-date=10 October 2014 |website=Bloomberg L.P. }}</ref> Two years later in 2014, Alibaba.com was privately held again following a $2.5 billion buyback.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Hsu |first=Tiffany |date=2012-05-25 |title=Alibaba.com shareholders OK $2.5 billion privatization bid |url=https://www.latimes.com/business/la-xpm-2012-may-25-la-fi-mo-alibaba-20120525-story.html |access-date=2026-01-09 |website=Los Angeles Times |language=en-US}}</ref> In 2013, 1688.com launched a direct channel that was responsible for $30&nbsp;million in daily transaction value.<ref name="hsu0713">{{Cite web |last=Hsu |first=Alex |date=17 July 2013 |title=Alibaba's 1688.com Launches New Direct Channel, Responsible for 30 Million USD Per Day in Transactions Value |url=http://bw-original-reporting.tumblr.com/post/55694392054/alibabas-1688-com-launches-new-direct-channel |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131013134423/http://bw-original-reporting.tumblr.com/post/55694392054/alibabas-1688-com-launches-new-direct-channel |archive-date=13 October 2013 |website=BrightWire News }}</ref>

In 2003, Alibaba launched Taobao Marketplace (淘宝网), offering a variety of products for retail sales. Taobao grew to become China's largest [[Customer to customer|C2C]] online shopping platform and later became the second most visited website in China, according to [[Alexa Internet]].<ref name="einhorn2013">{{Cite news |date=16 January 2013 |title=Alibaba Jack Ma Is Moving On, With an IPO Looming |work=[[Bloomberg L.P.|Bloomberg]] |publisher=Bruce Einhorn |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2013-01-15/alibabas-jack-ma-is-moving-on-with-an-ipo-looming |url-status=live |access-date=16 January 2013 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181002064603/https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2013-01-15/alibabas-jack-ma-is-moving-on-with-an-ipo-looming |archive-date=2 October 2018 }}</ref><ref name="Alexa.com-taobao">{{Cite web |title=Taobao's Alexa Statistics |url=http://www.alexa.com/siteinfo/taobao.com |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20090407040244/http://www.alexa.com/siteinfo/taobao.com |archive-date=7 April 2009 |access-date=8 January 2015 |website=Alexa.com }}</ref> Taobao's growth was attributed to offering free registration and commission-free transactions using a free third-party payment platform.<ref name="newschinamag1">{{Cite web |last=Zhe |first=The Sun |date=January 2012 |title=From Stall to Mall |url=http://www.newschinamag.com/magazine/from-stall-to-mall |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140925203642/http://www.newschinamag.com/magazine/from-stall-to-mall |archive-date=25 September 2014 |website=News China }}</ref> Advertising made up 75 percent of the company's total revenue, allowing it to break even in 2009. In 2010, Taobao's profit was estimated to be ¥1.5&nbsp;billion (US$235.7&nbsp;million), which was only about 0.4 percent of their total sales figure of ¥400&nbsp;billion (US$62.9&nbsp;billion) that year, way below the industry average of 2 percent, according to iResearch estimates.<ref name="newschinamag1" /> According to Zhang Yu, the director of Taobao, between 2011 and 2013, the number of stores on Taobao with annual sales under ¥100&nbsp;thousand increased by 60%; the number of stores with sales between ¥10&nbsp;thousand and ¥1&nbsp;million increased by 30%, and the number of stores with sales over ¥1&nbsp;million increased by 33%.<ref name="hsu0713b">{{Cite web |last=Hsu |first=Alex |date=17 July 2013 |title=Alibaba's Taobao Continues to Attract New Stores, Focuses on Personalized Services, Store Branding and Customized Contracts |url=http://bw-original-reporting.tumblr.com/post/55694291348/alibabas-taobao-continues-to-attract-new-stores |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131013140057/http://bw-original-reporting.tumblr.com/post/55694291348/alibabas-taobao-continues-to-attract-new-stores |archive-date=13 October 2013 |website=BrightWire News }}</ref>

In April 2008, Taobao introduced a spin-off, [[Tmall|Taobao Mall]] (淘宝商城, later Tmall.com), an online retail platform to complement the Taobao C2C portal, offering global brands to an increasingly affluent Chinese consumer base. It became the eighth most visited web site in China as of 2013.<ref name="Alexa.com-tmall">{{Cite web |title=Alexa Web Site Statistics for Tmall.com |url=http://www.alexa.com/siteinfo/tmall.com |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201218055903/https://www.alexa.com/siteinfo/tmall.com |archive-date=18 December 2020 |access-date=18 October 2013 |publisher=Alexa.com }}</ref> In 2012, Tmall.com later changed its Chinese name to Tianmao (天猫, "sky cat"), reflecting Tmall's Chinese pronunciation.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Tmall changes its name for better positioning - People's Daily Online |url=http://en.people.cn/90778/7702962.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190207112225/http://en.people.cn/90778/7702962.html |archive-date=7 February 2019 |access-date=3 January 2019 |website=en.people.cn }}</ref> In March 2010, Taobao launched the group shopping website Juhuasuan (聚划算), offering "flash sales", which are products that are available at a discount for only a fixed time period. In October 2010, Taobao beta-launched eTao, a [[comparison shopping website]] that offers search results from mostly Chinese online shopping platforms,<ref>{{Cite web |date=13 October 2010 |title=Microsoft and eTao |url=https://thenextweb.com/asia/2010/10/13/microsoft-and-alibaba-launch-search-engine-etao-in-china/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190327101611/https://thenextweb.com/asia/2010/10/13/microsoft-and-alibaba-launch-search-engine-etao-in-china/ |archive-date=27 March 2019 |access-date=10 December 2015 |website=The Next Web }}</ref> including product searches, sales and coupon searches. According to the Alibaba Group web site, eTao offers products from Amazon China, [[Dangdang]], Gome, [[Yihaodian]], [[Nike, Inc.|Nike]] China, and Vancl, as well as Taobao and Tmall.<ref name=home/> As part of a restructuring of [[Taobao]] by Alibaba, these spin-offs became separate companies in 2011, with Tmall and eTao becoming separate businesses in June and Juhuasuan becoming a separate business later in October.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Pepitone |first=Julianne |date=17 June 2011 |title=Alibaba splits Taobao into three parts |work=[[CNN]] |url=https://money.cnn.com/2011/06/17/technology/taobao_split/index.htm |url-status=dead |access-date=3 January 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190104072852/https://money.cnn.com/2011/06/17/technology/taobao_split/index.htm |archive-date=4 January 2019 }}</ref>

In 2010, Alibaba launched AliExpress.com, an online retail service made up of mostly small Chinese businesses offering products to international online buyers. It is the most visited e-commerce platform in Russia.<ref>{{Cite web |author=Adrien Henni |date=9 September 2014 |title=Alibaba tops e-commerce sites in Russia as well as China |url=http://www.internetretailer.com/commentary/2014/09/09/alibaba-tops-e-commerce-sites-russia-well-china |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141028003039/http://www.internetretailer.com/commentary/2014/09/09/alibaba-tops-e-commerce-sites-russia-well-china |archive-date=28 October 2014 |access-date=9 May 2017 |publisher=Internetretailer.com }}</ref> It allows small businesses in China to sell to customers all over the world, resulting in a wide variety of products. It might be more accurate to compare AliExpress to eBay, though, as sellers are independent; it simply serves as a host for other businesses to sell to consumers.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Kasperkevic |first=Jana |date=23 September 2014 |title=Why Alibaba is a success in the US: its market for human hair extensions nets American women in its weave |language=en-GB |work=The Guardian |url=https://www.theguardian.com/money/us-money-blog/2014/sep/23/alibaba-human-hair-american-women-ecommerce |url-status=live |access-date=5 May 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190402141905/https://www.theguardian.com/money/us-money-blog/2014/sep/23/alibaba-human-hair-american-women-ecommerce |archive-date=2 April 2019 |issn=0261-3077 }}</ref> Similar to eBay, sellers on Aliexpress can be either companies or individuals. It connects Chinese businesses directly with buyers. The main difference from Taobao is that it's aimed primarily at international buyers, mainly the US, Russia, Brazil and Spain.<ref>{{Cite news |title=Jack Ma: Su inspiradora historia |language=es-ES |work=GOOALI |url=https://www.gooali.com/blogs/aliexpress/82661636-jack-ma-su-inspiradora-historia |url-status=live |access-date=5 May 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181023075128/https://www.gooali.com/blogs/aliexpress/82661636-jack-ma-su-inspiradora-historia |archive-date=23 October 2018 }}</ref>

In 2012, Alibaba instituted its Public Dispute Resolution Center, which crowdsources buyers and sellers to vote on transactional rules and decide the outcome of disputes on the platform.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=215}} This decentralized approach was popular and similar mechanisms were adopted or experimented with by other e-commerce platforms in China.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|pages=215-216}} 

{{anchor|11Main}} On 11 June 2014, Alibaba launched US shopping site 11 Main. The 11 Main marketplace hosts merchants in categories such as clothing, fashion accessories and jewelry as well as interior goods and arts and crafts and it plans to keep adding more, said the company.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Osawa |first=Juro |date=11 June 2014 |title=Alibaba Tackles Amazon, eBay on Home Turf |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-launches-u-s-shopping-site-11-main-1402469675 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140929224442/http://online.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-launches-u-s-shopping-site-11-main-1402469675 |archive-date=29 September 2014 }}</ref><ref name="bbc">{{Cite news |date=11 June 2014 |title=China's Alibaba plans US online shop |work=BBC News |publisher=BBC |url=https://www.bbc.com/news/business-27790367 |url-status=live |access-date=11 July 2014 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190506033852/https://www.bbc.com/news/business-27790367 |archive-date=6 May 2019 }}</ref> On 23 June 2015, Alibaba announced that it is selling 11 Main to OpenSky, an online-marketplace operator based in New York.<ref>{{Cite news |last1=Osawa |first1=Juro |last2=Dou |first2=Eva |date=23 June 2015 |title=Alibaba Stumbles in U.S. Online Market |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-sells-u-s-subsidiary-11-main-to-opensky-1435027883 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=5 March 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190313184641/https://www.wsj.com/articles/alibaba-sells-u-s-subsidiary-11-main-to-opensky-1435027883 |archive-date=13 March 2019 }}</ref>
[[File:Lazada Laguna warehouse.jpg|thumb|[[Lazada Group|Lazada]] warehouse in [[Cabuyao, Laguna]], Philippines during the company's 11.11 sale promotion in 2018. Lazada Group is a subsidiary of Alibaba Group and Alibaba co-founder [[Peng Lei|Lucy Peng Lei]] is CEO of the company.]]

In February 2015, Alibaba invests US$590&nbsp;million in [[Meizu]], acquiring an undisclosed minority stake.<ref>{{Cite news |date=9 February 2015 |title=Alibaba Invests $590M In Smartphone Maker Meizu |publisher=chinamoneynetwork |url=https://www.chinamoneynetwork.com/2015/02/09/alibaba-invest-590m-in-smartphone-maker-meizu/amp |url-status=live |access-date=9 February 2015 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210822171951/https://www.chinamoneynetwork.com/2015/02/09/alibaba-invest-590m-in-smartphone-maker-meizu/amp |archive-date=22 August 2021 }}</ref>

In 2016, Alibaba acquired control of Singapore-based e-commerce platform [[Lazada]].<ref name=":Han" />{{Rp|page=49}} Lazada is the largest e-commerce platform in southeast Asia, with a major presence in Malaysia, the Philippines, Singapore, Thailand, and Vietnam.<ref name=":Han" />{{Rp|page=49}} This acquisition increased integration on Alibaba between Chinese merchants and their customers in southeast Asia.<ref name=":Han" />{{Rp|page=50}}

In October 2016, Alibaba launched Alitrip, later named Fliggy, an online travel platform that is designed as an online mall for brands such as airline companies and agencies.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Fox |first=Linda |date=3 July 2017 |title=Fliggy, the online travel platform you (probably) haven't heard of |work=Phocuswire |url=https://www.phocuswire.com/Fliggy-the-online-travel-platform-you-probably-havent-heard-of |url-status=live |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180925234606/https://www.tnooz.com/article/fliggy-online-travel-platform-China/ |archive-date=25 September 2018 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |date=26 January 2018 |title=5 reasons why travel brands need to take notice of Alibaba's Fliggy |work=ChinaTravelNews |url=https://www.chinatravelnews.com/article/120020 |url-status=live |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181204010333/https://www.chinatravelnews.com/article/120020 |archive-date=4 December 2018 }}</ref> Fliggy set the target audience as the younger generation and it strives to become a one-stop service when they plan their trips, particularly in overseas travel.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba Relaunches Travel site as Fliggy to Compete with CTrip |url=http://www.inboundreport.com/2017/07/11/alibaba-relaunches-travel-site-as-fliggy-to-compete-with-ctrip/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181016160502/http://www.inboundreport.com/2017/07/11/alibaba-relaunches-travel-site-as-fliggy-to-compete-with-ctrip/ |archive-date=16 October 2018 |access-date=7 March 2018 |website=Inbound Reporter |date=11 July 2017 |publisher=Tom Berrigan }}</ref> On 7 August 2017, Alibaba Group and [[Marriott International]] hotel group announced a comprehensive strategic co-operation. Two companies will set up a joint venture company. Through the docking technology system and the superiority resources, Fliggy has Marriott hotel flagship store. It has the same function with Marriott Chinese website and Marriott mobile app to create the best global travel experience for consumers.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Poling |first=Monica |date=8 August 2017 |title=Marriott Targets Chinese Travelers via Alibaba |work=[[TravelPulse]] |url=https://www.travelpulse.com/news/hotels-and-resorts/marriott-targets-chinese-travelers-via-alibaba.html |url-status=live |access-date=15 March 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180316151714/https://www.travelpulse.com/news/hotels-and-resorts/marriott-targets-chinese-travelers-via-alibaba.html |archive-date=16 March 2018 }}</ref>

In 2016, the [[Office of the United States Trade Representative]] added Taobao back onto a list of notorious counterfeit platforms that includes the likes of torrent site [[The Pirate Bay]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Cendrowski |first=Scott |date=22 December 2016 |title=Why Alibaba Can't Complain About Its Return to the 'Notorious' Counterfeit Market List |work=[[Fortune (magazine)|Fortune]] |url=https://fortune.com/2016/12/22/alibaba-taobao-counterfeit-goods-platform/ |url-status=live |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191004131746/https://fortune.com/2016/12/22/alibaba-taobao-counterfeit-goods-platform/ |archive-date=4 October 2019 }}</ref> Alibaba denied wrongdoing and filed two lawsuits against the counterfeiters {{as of|2017|January|4|lc=y}},<ref>{{Cite news |last=Kharpal |first=Arjun |date=4 January 2017 |title=Alibaba sues sellers of counterfeit good for the first time after it was blacklisted by the US |work=[[CNBC]] |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2017/01/04/alibaba-sues-sellers-of-counterfeit-goods-for-the-first-time-after-it-was-blacklisted-by-the-us.html |url-status=live |access-date=8 September 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170506002246/http://www.cnbc.com/2017/01/04/alibaba-sues-sellers-of-counterfeit-goods-for-the-first-time-after-it-was-blacklisted-by-the-us.html |archive-date=6 May 2017 }}</ref> but brands whose sales have been affected by the counterfeit products accused Alibaba of not doing enough.<ref>{{Cite news |date=4 February 2017 |title=Brands accuse Alibaba of not doing enough to stop sale of counterfeit goods |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/brands-accuse-alibaba-of-not-doing-enough-to-stop-sale-of-counterfeit-goods-1475898037 |url-status=live |access-date=16 February 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170216221030/https://www.wsj.com/articles/brands-accuse-alibaba-of-not-doing-enough-to-stop-sale-of-counterfeit-goods-1475898037 |archive-date=16 February 2017 }}</ref>

In 2017, Alibaba started opening a chain of supermarkets, named Freshippo (or Hema; 盒马), as part of the company's "new retail strategy," where customers can either order in the store or online for delivery in under 30 minutes. It offers a mobile app that recommends customers products based on data analytics.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Choudhury |first=Saheli Roy |date=18 July 2017 |title=Alibaba Hema stores blend online and offline retail |work=[[CNBC]] |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2017/07/18/alibaba-hema-stores-blend-online-and-offline-retail.html |url-status=live |access-date=29 April 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190323161223/https://www.cnbc.com/2017/07/18/alibaba-hema-stores-blend-online-and-offline-retail.html |archive-date=23 March 2019 }}</ref> In addition, customers can have their groceries cooked to eat in the food court of the supermarket.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Peterson |first=Hayley |date=3 February 2018 |title=China has a supermarket unlike anything in the US – and it has 2 major advantages over Amazon Go |work=[[Business Insider]] |url=https://www.businessinsider.com/chinas-hema-market-has-two-advantages-over-amazon-go-2018-2 |url-status=live |access-date=29 April 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190301170416/https://www.businessinsider.com/chinas-hema-market-has-two-advantages-over-amazon-go-2018-2 |archive-date=1 March 2019 }}</ref>

In October 2018, it was reported that Alibaba's Koubei have merged with online food delivery service platform [[Ele.me]] into a new local life service subsidiary.<ref>{{Cite news |date=23 June 2015 |title=Alibaba, Ant to pour nearly $1 billion into local services startup |language=en |work=Reuters |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-koubei-idUSKBN0P307Q20150623 |url-status=live |access-date=11 January 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220111053319/https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-koubei-idUSKBN0P307Q20150623 |archive-date=11 January 2022 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=12 October 2018 |title=Ele.me and Koubei Merged to Become Alibaba's New Local Life Service Company |url=https://pandaily.com/ele-me-and-koubei-merged-to-become-alibabas-new-local-life-service-company/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220111053328/https://pandaily.com/ele-me-and-koubei-merged-to-become-alibabas-new-local-life-service-company/ |archive-date=11 January 2022 |access-date=11 January 2022 |website=Pandaily |language=en-US }}</ref> However, the newly formed Alibaba Local Life Service entity experienced major competition from local service giant [[Meituan]], which is backed by [[Tencent]], leading to rumours of planned layoffs in 2022.<ref>{{Cite web |date=5 January 2022 |title=Alibaba's local consumer businesses reportedly plan layoffs as they lose ground to Meituan |url=https://kr-asia.com/alibabas-local-consumer-businesses-reportedly-plan-layoffs-as-they-lose-ground-to-meituan |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220111053324/https://kr-asia.com/alibabas-local-consumer-businesses-reportedly-plan-layoffs-as-they-lose-ground-to-meituan |archive-date=11 January 2022 |access-date=11 January 2022 |website=KrASIA |language=en |author-first1=Jiaxing|author-last1=Li}}</ref>

In September 2019, Alibaba announced it would acquire Chinese e-commerce platform Kaola from [[NetEase]] for around {{USD|2 billion}}.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Choudhury |first=Saheli Roy |date=6 September 2019 |title=Alibaba buys Chinese e-commerce business Kaola for $2 billion |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2019/09/06/alibaba-baba-to-buy-e-commerce-unit-kaola-from-netease-for-2-billion.html |access-date=12 October 2023 |website=CNBC |language=en }}</ref> In October 2020, Alibaba agreed to pay US$3.6&nbsp;billion to take control of China's biggest hypermarket operator Sun Art (SEHK: 6808) from French billionaire [[Mulliez family]]. The deal doubled the group's stake in the hypermarket chain with a total ownership of 72%.<ref>{{Cite web |date=19 October 2020 |title=Alibaba to pay US$3.6 billion for control of China's top hypermarket chain |url=https://www.scmp.com/business/china-business/article/3106045/alibaba-group-pay-us36-billion-take-control-chinas-biggest |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201023131132/https://www.scmp.com/business/china-business/article/3106045/alibaba-group-pay-us36-billion-take-control-chinas-biggest |archive-date=23 October 2020 |access-date=24 October 2020 |website=South China Morning Post |language=en |author-last1=Liu |author-first1=Yujing }}</ref>

====Gold Supplier membership====
Alibaba.com offers a paid Gold Supplier membership to try to ensure that each seller is genuine; sellers' Gold Supplier status and the number of years it has been held are displayed.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Gold Supplier |url=https://www.alibaba.com/help/gold_supplier.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200121094437/https://www.alibaba.com/help/gold_supplier.html |archive-date=21 January 2020 |access-date=19 January 2020 |publisher=Alibaba Group }}</ref> The supplier verification types and checks are listed on Alibaba.com's website, with more stringent checks for sellers outside China.<ref name="goldchecks">{{Cite web |title=Alibaba.com's Supplier Verification Services |url=https://service.alibaba.com/buyer/ab/safety_security/products/verification_services.php |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190715135842/https://service.alibaba.com/buyer/ab/safety_security/products/verification_services.php |archive-date=15 July 2019 |access-date=19 January 2020 |publisher=Alibaba Group }}</ref> While the majority of suppliers are reported to be genuine,{{citation needed|date=August 2013}} there have been many cases of sellers, some with Gold Supplier status, seeking to defraud unsuspecting buyers.<ref name="minalto">{{Cite web |author=Andrew Minalto |date=January 2014 |title=Top 10 Scams on Alibaba.com! |url=http://andrewminalto.com/top-10-alibaba-scams/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160123142340/http://andrewminalto.com/top-10-alibaba-scams/ |archive-date=23 January 2016 |access-date=22 January 2016 |newspaper=Andrewminalto.com }}</ref> In February 2011, controversy ensued when Alibaba's corporate office admitted that it had granted the mark of integrity of its "China Gold Supplier" program to at least 2,000 dealers that had subsequently defrauded buyers; the firm's share price dropped "abruptly" after the announcement.<ref name="thieves">{{Cite news |date=24 February 2011 |title=Alibaba and the 2,236 thieves |newspaper=[[The Economist]] |url=https://www.economist.com/business/2011/02/24/alibaba-and-the-2236-thieves |url-status=live |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200103034717/https://www.economist.com/business/2011/02/24/alibaba-and-the-2236-thieves |archive-date=3 January 2020 }}</ref> A statement from the firm reported that Yan Limin, the general manager of Alibaba.com at the time, had been dismissed in March for "misconduct"; Phil Muncaster of UK's ''The Register'' additionally reported that "a further 28 employees had been involved in dodgy dealings".<ref>{{Cite news |last=Muncaster |first=Phil |date=6 July 2012 |title=Ex-Alibaba GM cuffed as bribery scandal resurfaces |work=[[The Register]] |url=https://www.theregister.co.uk/2012/07/06/alibaba_general_manager_arrested_bribery |url-status=live |access-date=24 August 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171012150143/https://www.theregister.co.uk/2012/07/06/alibaba_general_manager_arrested_bribery |archive-date=12 October 2017 }}</ref>

As ''The Economist'' noted, the company's response has conflicting components: Alibaba's promulgated view that its corrective actions indicate its commitment to quality and integrity (where it contrasts itself with other scandal-associated Chinese business sectors), ''versus'' a damage control view suggesting that the subscription-driven, third-party verified "China Gold Supplier" program was endangered by diminished trust in its endorsement system, removing the incentive for global buyers to choose Alibaba as their business-to-business service, thus more broadly endangering Alibaba through impact on its brand and capabilities (the latter via the "defenestration of senior people").<ref name=thieves/> The scandal was said to have placed the head of Alibaba Group, Jack Ma—who was described as having been furious over the scandal—in a position to personally fight to win back trust.<ref>{{Cite journal |last=Epstein |first=Gady |date=11 April 2011 |title=Alibaba's Jack Ma Fights To Win Back Trust |url=https://www.forbes.com/forbes/2011/0411/features-jack-ma-alibaba-e-commerce-scandal-face-of-china.html |url-status=live |journal=Forbes |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20120907003642/http://www.forbes.com/forbes/2011/0411/features-jack-ma-alibaba-e-commerce-scandal-face-of-china.html |archive-date=7 September 2012 |access-date=23 September 2012 }}</ref>

===Smart logistics===
In 2013, Alibaba and six large Chinese logistics companies (including SF Express) established a company called [[Cainiao]] for delivery of packages in China. This network gradually grew to 14 local logistics companies in 2014.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Liu |first=Cynthia |date=7 December 2016 |title=One Platform to Rule Them All: The Brains Behind Cainiao Network (PI) |url=https://ecommerceiq.asia/cainiao-logistics-southeast-asia/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210816223920/https://ecommerceiq.asia/cainiao-logistics-southeast-asia/ |archive-date=16 August 2021 |access-date=17 December 2018 |website=E Commerce Asia |publisher=E COm Asia }}</ref> In 2016, Alibaba's [[Taobao]] and [[Tmall]], two of the world's largest and most popular online retail marketplaces, achieved a total transaction volume of 3&nbsp;trillion yuan (US$478.6&nbsp;billion). The company aims to double the transaction volume to 6&nbsp;trillion yuan by 2020. {{as of|2018|February}}, Taobao reached 580 million [[monthly active users]], while Tmall achieved 500 million monthly active users.<ref>{{Cite web |date=19 January 2017 |title=22 Amazing Taobao Statistics |url=https://expandedramblings.com/index.php/taobao-statistics/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180611034415/https://expandedramblings.com/index.php/taobao-statistics/ |archive-date=11 June 2018 |access-date=5 March 2018 |website=DMR }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=19 February 2017 |title=18 Amazing Tmall Statistics |url=https://expandedramblings.com/index.php/tmall-statistics/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180620234925/https://expandedramblings.com/index.php/tmall-statistics/ |archive-date=20 June 2018 |access-date=5 March 2018 |website=DMR }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Lee |first=Cyrus |title=Alibaba achieves 3 trillion yuan transaction volume milestone |language=en |work=ZDNet |url=https://www.zdnet.com/article/alibaba-achieves-3-trillion-yuan-transaction-volume-milestone/ |url-status=live |access-date=22 January 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180326230430/http://www.zdnet.com/article/alibaba-achieves-3-trillion-yuan-transaction-volume-milestone/ |archive-date=26 March 2018 }}</ref> It is also rapidly expanding its e-commerce network abroad.<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Wu |first1=Kane |last2=Cadell |first2=Cate |date=26 September 2017 |editor=Muralikumar Anantharaman |title=Alibaba takes control of logistics business, pledges $15 billion to expand network |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-cainiao-investment/alibaba-takes-control-of-logistics-business-pledges-15-billion-to-expand-network-idUSKCN1C10C2 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181003122826/https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-cainiao-investment/alibaba-takes-control-of-logistics-business-pledges-15-billion-to-expand-network-idUSKCN1C10C2 |archive-date=3 October 2018 |access-date=12 October 2017 |website=[[Reuters]] }}</ref> Alibaba has also announced that it will invest 100&nbsp;billion yuan over five years to build a global logistics network, underpinning an aggressive overseas expansion, and demonstrating Alibaba's commitment to building the most efficient logistics network in China and around the world. It is investing a further 5.3&nbsp;billion yuan in [[Cainiao]] Logistics to boost its stake to 51 percent from 47 percent.<ref>{{Cite web |date=26 September 2017 |title=Alibaba to Take Majority Stake in Cainiao Network |url=http://www.alibabagroup.com/en/news/article?news=p170926 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171003041151/http://www.alibabagroup.com/en/news/article?news=p170926 |archive-date=3 October 2017 |access-date=3 October 2017 |website=Alibaba Group |language=en }}</ref> The investment would value Cainiao, a joint venture of top Chinese logistics firms, at around US$20&nbsp;billion.<ref>{{Cite web |author=Hermes Auto |date=27 September 2017 |title=Alibaba takes control of logistics business |url=https://www.straitstimes.com/business/companies-markets/alibaba-takes-control-of-logistics-business |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181219134310/https://www.straitstimes.com/business/companies-markets/alibaba-takes-control-of-logistics-business |archive-date=19 December 2018 |access-date=19 December 2018 |website=The Straits Times }}</ref>

Alibaba uses [[Cainiao]] software at its Belgian-European sales hub. The software is able to access data about retailers, products, transport details and flows, a person familiar with the company's IT systems told the British newspaper ''[[Financial Times]]''. Chinese companies are legally obliged to pass on their data to China's authorities and security services.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Belgischer Geheimdienst überwacht Logistikzentrum von Alibaba |url=https://www.tagesschau.de/wirtschaft/weltwirtschaft/alibaba-china-technologie-spionage-100.html |access-date=5 October 2023 |website=tagesschau.de |language=de }}</ref> The Belgium [[State Security Service (Belgium)|State Security Service]] is monitoring the hub in Lütich because of this fact and possible [[Chinese intelligence activity abroad|espionage]].<ref>{{Cite news |date=6 October 2023 |title=Belgian intelligence investigating logistics hub of China's Alibaba at local airport |language=en-GB |work=[[The Guardian]] |agency=[[Reuters]] |url=https://www.theguardian.com/business/2023/oct/06/belgian-intelligence-investigating-logistics-hub-of-chinas-alibaba-at-local-airport |access-date=7 October 2023 |issn=0261-3077 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=5 October 2023 |title=Belgium monitoring China's Alibaba over spying concerns |url=https://www.euronews.com/2023/10/05/belgium-monitoring-chinas-alibaba-over-spying-concerns |access-date=5 October 2023 |website=[[Euronews]] |language=en |archive-date=6 October 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231006015627/https://www.euronews.com/2023/10/05/belgium-monitoring-chinas-alibaba-over-spying-concerns |url-status=live }}</ref> According to the ''Financial Times'', Cainiao denied suggestions of wrongdoing, noting that the firm operates in compliance with relevant laws and regulations.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Dubois |first1=Laura |last2=Liu |first2=Qianer |date=5 October 2023 |title=Alibaba accused of 'possible espionage' at European hub |work=[[Financial Times]] |publisher= |url=https://www.ft.com/content/256ee824-9710-49d2-a8bc-f173e3f74286 |url-access=subscription |access-date=24 October 2023 |archive-date=23 October 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231023215104/https://www.ft.com/content/256ee824-9710-49d2-a8bc-f173e3f74286 |url-status=live }}</ref>

According to the ''Reuters'', Alibaba Group Holding Ltd has divested its logistics arm for $8.5 billion to a consortium led by investment firms [[Boyu Capital]] and [[Primavera Capital Group]].<ref>{{cite news |date=26 March 2024 |title=Alibaba to buy Cainiao stake for up to $3.75 billion as it drops IPO plan |work=[[Reuters]] |publisher= |url=https://www.reuters.com/markets/asia/alibaba-dumps-logistics-arm-ipo-buy-rest-cainiao-stake-up-38-bln-2024-03-26/ |url-access=subscription |access-date=26 March 2024 }}</ref>

=== Cloud computing and artificial intelligence technology ===
In conjunction with the company's 10th anniversary, Alibaba launched [[Alibaba Cloud]] in September 2009, aiming to build a cloud computing service platform, including e-commerce data mining, e-commerce data processing, and data customization.<ref name=home/> It has R&D centres and operators in Hangzhou, Beijing, Hong Kong, Singapore, Silicon Valley<ref>{{Cite web |title=aliyun.com |url=http://www.aliyun.com/about/?spm=5176.383338.25.1.rpQhdR |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140819234144/http://www.aliyun.com/about/?spm=5176.383338.25.1.rpQhdR |archive-date=19 August 2014 |access-date=19 August 2014 }}</ref> and Dubai.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Wanxia |first=Lin |date=22 November 2016 |title=Alibaba Cloud's global expansion program rolls into Dubai |url=https://asiatimes.com/article/alibaba-clouds-global-expansion-program-rolls-dubai/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215064802/https://asiatimes.com/2016/11/alibaba-clouds-global-expansion-program-rolls-dubai/ |archive-date=15 February 2022 |access-date=2 December 2016 |website=www.atimes.com }}</ref> In July 2014, Alibaba Cloud entered into a partnership deal with Inspur. Alibaba Cloud is the largest high-end cloud computing company in China.<ref>{{Cite web |date=7 July 2014 |title=Aliyun & Inspur Partner to Beat Cloud competition |url=http://www.cloudwedge.com/2014-aliyun-inspur-partner-to-beat-cloud-competition/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140819232901/http://www.cloudwedge.com/2014-aliyun-inspur-partner-to-beat-cloud-competition/ |archive-date=19 August 2014 |access-date=19 August 2014 }}</ref> In 2009, Alibaba acquired HiChina, the largest domain registration service and web hosting service company in China, and built it into Alibaba Cloud.<ref name="HiChina">{{Cite news |last=Mutton |first=Paul |date=27 May 2015 |title=Aliyun cloud growth makes Alibaba largest hosting company in China |publisher=Netcraft |url=http://news.netcraft.com/archives/2015/05/27/aliyun-cloud-growth-makes-alibaba-largest-hosting-company-in-china.html |url-status=live |access-date=17 August 2016 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190424002845/https://news.netcraft.com/archives/2015/05/27/aliyun-cloud-growth-makes-alibaba-largest-hosting-company-in-china.html |archive-date=24 April 2019 }}</ref> On 28 July 2011, Alibaba Cloud released [[AliOS]] (formerly ''Yun OS'' and ''Aliyun OS''), a [[Linux distribution]] designed for mobile devices.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Vaughan-Nichols |first=Steven J. |title=Is Aliyun OS really Linux? Android? A rip-off of both? |url=https://www.zdnet.com/article/is-aliyun-os-really-linux-android-a-rip-off-of-both/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180511102439/https://www.zdnet.com/article/is-aliyun-os-really-linux-android-a-rip-off-of-both/ |archive-date=11 May 2018 |access-date=19 December 2018 |website=ZDNet }}</ref> In the 2017 Computing Conference in Hangzhou, Alibaba launched [[AliGenie]], a China-based open-platform intelligent personal assistant. It is currently used in the [[Tmall Genie]] smart speaker.<ref>{{Cite web |date=11 August 2017 |title=Alibaba's AI-powered speaker hits the shelves · TechNode |url=https://technode.com/2017/08/11/alibabas-first-consumer-targeting-artificial-intelligence-speaker-genie-x1-unveils/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170921011939/http://technode.com/2017/08/11/alibabas-first-consumer-targeting-artificial-intelligence-speaker-genie-x1-unveils/ |archive-date=21 September 2017 |access-date=19 December 2018 }}</ref>

In 2018, Alibaba's Wanli Min presented [[City Brain]],<ref>{{Cite web |date=19 October 2018 |title=Alibaba's Wanli Min presents 'City Brain' and talks upcoming quarterly results |work=euronews |url=https://www.euronews.com/2018/10/19/alibaba-s-wanli-min-presents-city-brain-and-talks-upcoming-quarterly-results |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200604103925/https://www.euronews.com/2018/10/19/alibaba-s-wanli-min-presents-city-brain-and-talks-upcoming-quarterly-results |archive-date=4 June 2020 |access-date=4 June 2020 }}</ref> a technology geared towards urban solutions such as streamlining traffic, detecting accidents and improving transport efficiency.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Alibaba's 'City Brain' is slashing congestion in its hometown |date=15 January 2019 |url=https://edition.cnn.com/2019/01/15/tech/alibaba-city-brain-hangzhou/index.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200604103927/https://edition.cnn.com/2019/01/15/tech/alibaba-city-brain-hangzhou/index.html |archive-date=4 June 2020 |access-date=4 June 2020 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=City Brain |url=https://www.alibabacloud.com/solutions/intelligence-brain/city |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200603170824/https://www.alibabacloud.com/solutions/intelligence-brain/city |archive-date=3 June 2020 |access-date=4 June 2020 }}</ref>

China's government designated Alibaba as one of its "AI [[National champions|champions]]" in 2018.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=281}}

On 27 July 2019, Alibaba unveiled a [[64-bit computing|64-bit]] [[RISC-V]] [[Central processing unit|processor]] called the XuanTie-910 (Black Iron 910). It is a [[14 nanometer|12 nm]] [[Multi-core processor|16-core]] with a clockrate of 2.5&nbsp;GHz, and was designed by Alibaba's subsidiary [[T-Head]] (also known as Pingtouge). Alibaba claim the Xuantie-910 is faster than the [[ARM Cortex-A73]] and is capable of 7.1 Coremark/MHz.<ref>{{Cite web |date=26 July 2019 |title=Alibaba Unveils Open Source RISC-V CPU Amid US-China Trade War |url=https://www.tomshardware.com/news/alibaba-risc-v-cpu-processor-xuantie-910,40027.html |access-date=27 September 2019 |website=Tom's Hardware |language=en |archive-date=15 February 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215064808/https://www.tomshardware.com/news/alibaba-risc-v-cpu-processor-xuantie-910,40027.html |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=25 July 2019 |title=Alibaba Is Open-Sourcing Its Powerful New RISC-V Processor for 5G and AI |url=https://syncedreview.com/2019/07/25/alibaba-is-open-sourcing-its-powerful-new-risc-v-processor-for-5g-and-ai/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211124040030/https://syncedreview.com/2019/07/25/alibaba-is-open-sourcing-its-powerful-new-risc-v-processor-for-5g-and-ai/ |archive-date=24 November 2021 |access-date=27 September 2019 |website=Synced |language=en-US }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Quach |first=Katyanna |date=27 July 2019 |title=Alibaba sketches world's 'fastest' 'open-source' RISC-V processor yet: 16 cores, 64-bit, 2.5GHz, 12nm, out-of-order exec |url=https://www.theregister.co.uk/2019/07/27/alibaba_risc_v_chip/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200429004606/https://www.theregister.co.uk/2019/07/27/alibaba_risc_v_chip/ |archive-date=29 April 2020 |access-date=27 September 2019 |website=The Register |language=en }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Cutress |first=Dr Ian |title=Hot Chips 2020 Live Blog: Alibaba Xuantie-910 RISC-V CPU (3:00pm PT) |url=https://www.anandtech.com/show/15991/hot-chips-2020-live-blog-alibaba-xuantie910-riscv-cpu-300pm-pt |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200818075244/https://www.anandtech.com/show/15991/hot-chips-2020-live-blog-alibaba-xuantie910-riscv-cpu-300pm-pt |archive-date=18 August 2020 |access-date=20 August 2020 |website=www.anandtech.com }}</ref>

On 25 September 2019, Alibaba announced an [[AI accelerator]] called the Hanguang 800. The Hanguang 800 contains 17&nbsp;billion transistors built with a 12 nm process and was designed by T-Head and DAMO Academy (Alibaba's research institute). Alibaba claim it is capable of 78,563 images per second (IPS) inference and 500 IPS/W in ResNet-50. Alibaba plans to lease Hanguang 800 cloud usage.<ref>{{Cite news |date=25 September 2019 |title=Alibaba unveils self-developed AI chip for cloud computing services |language=en |work=Reuters |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-chip-idUSKBN1WA09R |url-status=live |access-date=27 September 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210818145843/https://www.reuters.com/article/us-alibaba-chip-idUSKBN1WA09R |archive-date=18 August 2021 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |date=25 September 2019 |title=Alibaba's New AI Chip Can Process Nearly 80K Images Per Second |url=https://syncedreview.com/2019/09/25/alibabas-new-ai-chip-can-process-nearly-80k-images-per-second/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210817151058/https://syncedreview.com/2019/09/25/alibabas-new-ai-chip-can-process-nearly-80k-images-per-second/ |archive-date=17 August 2021 |access-date=27 September 2019 |website=Synced |language=en-US }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Kharpal |first=Arjun |date=25 September 2019 |title=Alibaba unveils its first A.I. chip as China pushes for its own semiconductor technology |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2019/09/25/alibaba-unveils-its-first-ai-chip-called-the-hanguang-800.html |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20211027010649/https://www.cnbc.com/2019/09/25/alibaba-unveils-its-first-ai-chip-called-the-hanguang-800.html |archive-date=27 October 2021 |access-date=27 September 2019 |website=CNBC |language=en }}</ref>

{{Main|Qwen}}

In April 2023, Alibaba revealed its plans to release [[Qwen|Tongyi Qianwen]], a [[ChatGPT]]-like chatbot. Its name means "seeking an answer by asking a thousand questions". The company plans use Qwen in various business operations through existing services like DingTalk and Tmall Genie. The company received at least 200,000 requests from various businesses to join its beta testing program.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2023-04-26/alibaba-cutting-cloud-prices-as-much-as-50-to-drive-user-growth?sref=G4ZwBfC0 |title=Alibaba Cuts Cloud Prices to Spur Growth Before Possible IPO |publisher=Bloomberg |date=25 April 2023 }}</ref> Alibaba is also integrating Tongyi Qianwen into a digital assistant called Tingwu. Tongyi Tingwu, the AI-powered assistant, can analyze multimedia content and generate a text summary from video and audio files.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Kharpal |first=Arjun |date=1 June 2023 |title=Alibaba begins rollout of its ChatGPT-style tech as China A.I. race heats up |language=en-US |work=[[CNBC]] |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2023/06/01/alibaba-rolls-out-chatgpt-style-tech-as-china-ai-race-heats-up.html |archive-date=13 August 2023 |access-date=13 August 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230813010443/https://www.cnbc.com/2023/06/01/alibaba-rolls-out-chatgpt-style-tech-as-china-ai-race-heats-up.html |url-status=live }}</ref>

In November 2024, QwQ-32B-Preview, a model focusing on reasoning similar to OpenAI's [[OpenAI o1|o1]], was released under the [[Apache License#Apache License 2.0|Apache 2.0 License]], although only the weights were released, not the dataset or training method.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Dickson |first=Ben |date=2024-11-29 |title=Alibaba releases Qwen with Questions, an open reasoning model that beats o1-preview |url=https://venturebeat.com/ai/alibaba-releases-qwen-with-questions-an-open-reasoning-model-that-beats-o1-preview/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250118021329/https://venturebeat.com/ai/alibaba-releases-qwen-with-questions-an-open-reasoning-model-that-beats-o1-preview/ |archive-date=2025-01-18 |access-date=2026-04-02 |work=VentureBeat |language=en-US}}</ref>  This model, developed by Alibaba's Qwen team, can process prompts up to 32,000 words long and features a unique self-fact-checking mechanism that allows it to solve complex logic puzzles and mathematical problems.{{cn|date=November 2024}} The model is not without limitations, as it may unexpectedly switch languages, get stuck in reasoning loops, and struggle with common sense reasoning.{{cn|date=November 2024}} Notably, the model is available under an Apache 2.0 license on Hugging Face, making it commercially accessible, though only certain components have been released.{{cn|date=November 2024}} Its development comes amid growing skepticism about traditional AI scaling laws and represents a new approach to AI reasoning that involves giving models more processing time to complete tasks.{{cn|date=November 2024}}

In April 2025, Alibaba Cloud release Wan 2.1-FLF2V-14B Artificial Intelligence Video Generation Model under Apache license.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Wan-AI/Wan2.1-FLF2V-14B-720P · Hugging Face |url=https://huggingface.co/Wan-AI/Wan2.1-FLF2V-14B-720P |access-date=2025-04-21 |website=huggingface.co |archive-date=21 April 2025 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20250421000804/https://huggingface.co/Wan-AI/Wan2.1-FLF2V-14B-720P |url-status=live }}</ref>

==== Surveillance of Uyghurs ====
{{See also|Persecution of Uyghurs in China}}
In December 2020, ''[[The New York Times]]'' reported that Alibaba had developed and marketed facial recognition and surveillance software configured to detect [[Uyghurs|Uyghur]] faces and those of other [[ethnic minorities in China]].<ref>{{Cite news |last=Zhong |first=Raymond |date=16 December 2020 |title=As China Tracked Muslims, Alibaba Showed Customers How They Could, Too |language=en-US |work=[[The New York Times]] |url=https://www.nytimes.com/2020/12/16/technology/alibaba-china-facial-recognition-uighurs.html |access-date=17 December 2020 |issn=0362-4331 |archive-date=17 December 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201217025618/http://www.nytimes.com/2020/12/16/technology/alibaba-china-facial-recognition-uighurs.html |url-status=live }}</ref> Alibaba responded that they "do not and will not permit our technology to be used to target or identify specific ethnic groups" and that it was "dismayed to learn" that its Alibaba Cloud subsidiary had developed this feature,<ref>{{Cite news |date=18 December 2020 |title=Alibaba 'dismayed' that Alibaba Cloud developed feature allowing firms to identify Uighur minorities |work=[[Hong Kong Free Press]] |agency=[[Agence France-Presse]] |url=https://hongkongfp.com/2020/12/18/alibaba-dismayed-that-alibaba-cloud-developed-feature-allowing-firms-to-identify-uighur-minorities/ |access-date=18 December 2020 |archive-date=15 February 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220215064800/https://hongkongfp.com/2020/12/18/alibaba-dismayed-that-alibaba-cloud-developed-feature-allowing-firms-to-identify-uighur-minorities/ |url-status=live }}</ref> defending that the technology was developed "in a testing environment" and that it "was not deployed by any customer."<ref name=":IPVM Uyghur Recognition">{{cite news |title=Alibaba Admits Developing Racist Uyghur Recognition |url=https://ipvm.com/reports/alibaba-admits |work=IPVM |date=22 December 2020 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210713004306/https://ipvm.com/reports/alibaba-admits |archive-date=13 July 2021 |access-date=12 July 2021 }}</ref> However, [[IPVM]] reported in December 2020 that Alibaba refused to provide any proof the Uyghur recognition feature was just a "test" or "trial" and that Alibaba's own website showed Uyghur recognition as a live feature.<ref name=":IPVM Uyghur Recognition"/>

=== Entertainment services ===
In September 2017, Alibaba created a new live entertainment business unit under its '''Digital Media and Entertainment Group''' which focuses on ticketing, content creation and live experiences, bringing its entertainment ticketing platform ''Damai'' and its content creation and technology units ''MaiLive'' and ''Maizuo'' under one roof. It aims to provide a platform for live events (e.g. concerts, plays, [[esports]] and sports events), as well as supporting content partners and leveraging Alibaba's data capability for offline shows.<ref>{{Cite news |date=20 September 2017 |title=Alibaba announces live entertainment business group - TechNode |work=TechNode |url=http://technode.com/2017/09/20/alibaba-live-entertainment-business-group/ |url-status=live |access-date=21 September 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190402194201/https://technode.com/2017/09/20/alibaba-live-entertainment-business-group/ |archive-date=2 April 2019 }}</ref> It also provides the online digital distribution service 9Apps, which hosts downloadable content and applications.<ref>{{Cite news |title=Alibaba Owned UCWeb Faces Risk of Ban For Data Theft Allegations |work=inc42.com |url=https://inc42.com/buzz/alibaba-ucweb-data-theft/ |url-status=live |access-date=9 September 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190409190846/https://inc42.com/buzz/alibaba-ucweb-data-theft/ |archive-date=9 April 2019 }}</ref>

In March 2014, Alibaba agreed to acquire a controlling stake in [[Alibaba Pictures|ChinaVision Media Group]] for $804&nbsp;million. The two firms announced they would establish a strategic committee for potential future opportunities in online entertainment and other media areas.<ref>{{Cite web |last1=Kwok |first1=Donny |last2=Thomas |first2=Denny |last3=Carsten |first3=Paul |date=12 March 2014 |editor=Edwina Gibbs |title=Alibaba buys ChinaVision stake for $804&nbsp;million; gains TV, movie content |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/us-chinavision-alibaba/alibaba-buys-chinavision-stake-for-804-million-gains-tv-movie-content-idUSBREA2B02C20140312 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181204005749/https://www.reuters.com/article/us-chinavision-alibaba/alibaba-buys-chinavision-stake-for-804-million-gains-tv-movie-content-idUSBREA2B02C20140312 |archive-date=4 December 2018 |access-date=12 October 2017 |website=[[Reuters]] }}</ref> The company was renamed Alibaba Pictures Group (SEHK: 1060).<ref name="WSJ - ChinaVision">{{Cite news |last=Yuan |first=Li |date=16 November 2017 |title=Spoiler Alert: Jack Ma Beats Jet Li, but No Hollywood Ending for Alibaba |work=[[The Wall Street Journal]] |url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/spoiler-alert-jack-ma-beats-jet-li-but-no-hollywood-ending-for-alibaba-1510821204 |url-status=live |url-access=subscription |access-date=27 March 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180717210715/https://www.wsj.com/articles/spoiler-alert-jack-ma-beats-jet-li-but-no-hollywood-ending-for-alibaba-1510821204 |archive-date=17 July 2018 }}</ref> In March 2015, Alibaba Group launched [[AliMusic]] as its music division. Xiami Music and Tiantian Music are two of music streaming APP owned by AliMusic. AliMusic named [[Gao Xiaosong]] as the chairman and Song Ke as chief executive officer in July 2015.<ref>{{Cite news |date=20 July 2015 |title=Alibaba Names Renowned Musician Gao Xiaosong to Run Music Unit |language=en-US |work=Alizila |url=http://www.alizila.com/alibaba-names-renowned-musician-gao-xiaosong-run-music-unit-2/ |url-status=live |access-date=2 March 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180613103920/http://www.alizila.com/alibaba-names-renowned-musician-gao-xiaosong-run-music-unit-2/ |archive-date=13 June 2018 }}</ref> In 2017, Tencent Music has expected $10bn IPO by signing a rights deal with Alibaba, strengthening its position within the important Chinese market. Under the terms of the deal Alibaba will gain the right to stream music from international labels such as [[Sony Music]], [[Universal Music Group]] and [[YG Entertainment]], which already have exclusive deals with Tencent, in return for offering to its catalogue from [[Rock Records]], [[HIM International Music]] and so on.<ref>{{Cite news |date=12 September 2017 |title=Alibaba and Tencent collaborate on music copyright |language=en-US |work=TechNode |url=https://technode.com/2017/09/12/alibaba-and-tencent-collaborate-on-music-copyright/ |url-status=live |access-date=2 March 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808105528/https://technode.com/2017/09/12/alibaba-and-tencent-collaborate-on-music-copyright/ |archive-date=8 August 2018 }}</ref> In April 2014, Alibaba and [[Yunfeng Capital]], a private equity company controlled by Alibaba's founder, Jack Ma, agreed to acquire a combined 18.5 percent stake in [[Youku Tudou]], which broadcasts a series of popular television programs and other videos over the Internet.<ref>{{Cite web |author=Neil Gough |date=28 April 2014 |title=Alibaba Buys Stake in Chinese Web TV Company for $1.2 Billion |url=https://dealbook.nytimes.com/2014/04/28/alibaba-buying-stake-in-youku-tudou-a-chinese-web-tv-company-for-1-2-billion/?_php=true&_type=blogs |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181115112739/https://dealbook.nytimes.com/2014/04/28/alibaba-buying-stake-in-youku-tudou-a-chinese-web-tv-company-for-1-2-billion/?_php=true&_type=blogs |archive-date=15 November 2018 |access-date=5 March 2017 |website=The New York Times }}</ref> Alibaba acquired a stake in Youku Tudou for $1.22 billion in 2014.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://english.ckgsb.edu.cn/knowledge/article/china-roundup-alibabas-youku-tudou-stake-and-china-the-worlds-largest-economy/ |title=China Roundup: Alibaba's Youku Tudou Stake and China, the World's Largest Economy? |publisher=CKGSB Knowledge |access-date=2025-06-02 |language=en}}</ref> In its 2023 financial report, Alibaba disclosed a $1.2 billion impairment charge related to losses incurred by Youku.<ref>{{cite web |url=https://variety.com/2024/tv/news/alibaba-writes-youku-loss-making-streamer-1235900808/ |title=Alibaba Writes Down $1.2 Billion on Youku, Loss-Making Streamer, as Group Profits Plunge |date=7 February 2024 |publisher=Variety |access-date=2025-06-02 |language=en}}</ref>

Since restructuring in March 2023, the Digital Media and Entertainment Group has been led by Fan Luyuan as CEO.<ref>{{cite news |last1=Szalai |first1=Georg |title=Alibaba to Split Into 6 Parts, Including Digital Media and Entertainment Group |url=https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/business/business-news/alibaba-split-digital-media-entertainment-group-1235362094/ |access-date=25 November 2023 |work=The Hollywood Reporter |date=28 March 2023 |archive-date=25 November 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20231125205359/https://www.hollywoodreporter.com/business/business-news/alibaba-split-digital-media-entertainment-group-1235362094/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

In June 2025, Alibaba Pictures Group officially changed its name to Damai Entertainment Holdings Limited. Its business has expanded from film to a diversified ecosystem including performances, IP commercial derivatives, TV series, artist management, and ticketing platforms.<ref>{{Cite web |title=首页-大麦娱乐|url=https://www.damaiholdings.com/|access-date=2026-01-20 |website=Damai Entertainment}}</ref>

In April 2026, Alibaba launched an artificial intelligence model for game development and video generation, part of its broader efforts to expand its AI offerings and compete with rivals such as [[Tencent]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Ding |first=Luz |date=14 April 2026 |title=Alibaba Moves Onto Tencent's Turf With AI Model for 3D Video |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-04-16/alibaba-releases-new-ai-model-for-gaming-development |website=Bloomberg}}</ref>

=== Internet services ===
In 2004, the Alibaba Group released Aliwangwang, an instant messaging software service for interactions between customer and online sellers. By 2014, there are 50 million Aliwangwang users, making it the second-largest instant messaging tool in China.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Yan |first=Sophia |date=11 June 2014 |title=Alibaba continues quest to own the Chinese Internet |url=https://money.cnn.com/2014/06/11/technology/alibaba-ucweb/index.html |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200815022115/https://money.cnn.com/2014/06/11/technology/alibaba-ucweb/index.html |archive-date=15 August 2020 |access-date=9 May 2017 |publisher=CNN }}</ref> In October 2013, the Alibaba's chairman Jack Ma announced that the Alibaba Group would no longer use Tencent's messaging application [[WeChat]], and would henceforth promote its own messaging application and service, Laiwang.<ref>{{Cite news |date=23 October 2013 |title=Alibaba's Jack Ma to dump WeChat service in favour of 'own baby' Laiwang |work=[[South China Morning Post]] |url=https://www.scmp.com/news/china-insider/article/1337869/jack-ma-delete-wechat-account-favour-own-baby-laiwang |url-status=live |access-date=28 October 2013 In 2014, Alibaba Group founded [[DingTalk]], an enterprise communication and collaboration platform. Also known as Ding Ding, the app was developed as part of efforts to compete with rival Tencent's WeChat.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Li |first=Jane |date=10 March 2020 |title=What is DingTalk, Alibaba's Slack equivalent that quarantined kids in China hate? |url=https://qz.com/1814937/what-is-dingtalk-the-alibaba-app-that-quarantined-kids-in-china-hate/ |access-date=2 June 2022 |website=Quartz |language=en }}</ref> DingTalk is managed by Alibaba's cloud computing division.<ref>{{Cite web |date=30 May 2022 |title=Moderate growth amid a challenging landscape may be Alibaba's new normal |url=https://kr-asia.com/moderate-growth-amid-a-challenging-landscape-may-be-alibabas-new-normal |access-date=2 June 2022 |website=KrASIA |language=en |archive-date=31 May 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220531191522/https://kr-asia.com/moderate-growth-amid-a-challenging-landscape-may-be-alibabas-new-normal |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Qu |first=Tracy |date=27 May 2022 |title=Alibaba's first Cloud profit cheers investors amid broader e-commerce slowdown |url=https://www.scmp.com/tech/big-tech/article/3179481/alibabas-cloud-profit-provides-shard-light-slowing-economic-growth |url-access=subscription |access-date=2 June 2022 |website=South China Morning Post |language=en |archive-date=2 June 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220602011327/https://www.scmp.com/tech/big-tech/article/3179481/alibabas-cloud-profit-provides-shard-light-slowing-economic-growth |url-status=live }}</ref>

==== Yahoo! China ====
In October 2005, Alibaba Group formed a strategic partnership with Yahoo! and acquired Yahoo! China, a Chinese portal previously launched on 24 September 1999 that focuses on Internet services like news, email, and search.<ref>{{Cite news |date=11 August 2005 |title=Alibaba acquires Yahoo China |publisher=[[China Daily]] |agency=[[Xinhua News Agency]] |url=http://www.chinadaily.com.cn/english/doc/2005-08/11/content_468249.htm |url-status=live |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200408060604/http://www.chinadaily.com.cn/english/doc/2005-08/11/content_468249.htm |archive-date=8 April 2020 }}</ref> In 2005, Yahoo Inc. entered into a contract with Alibaba.com for $1 billion cash and gained 35% voting rights as the largest strategic investor. Alibaba also got rights to Yahoo's brand in China and together the entity was managed by Alibaba's chairman at the time Jack Ma.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Yahoo buys US$1 billion stake in Alibaba |url=https://www.chinadaily.com.cn/english/doc/2005-08/11/content_468252.htm |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260308233518/https://www.chinadaily.com.cn/english/doc/2005-08/11/content_468252.htm |archive-date=8 March 2026 |access-date=2026-06-04 |website=www.chinadaily.com.cn}}</ref>''' '''In April 2013, Alibaba Group announced that, as part of the agreement to buy back the Yahoo! Mail stake, that it would suspend technological support for China Yahoo! Mail service and begin migration of Yahoo! China Mail accounts. Several options were offered to users to make the transition as smooth as possible, and Yahoo! China users had four months to migrate their accounts to the Aliyun mail service, the [[Yahoo! Mail]] service in the United States, or to another third-party e-mail provider of the user's choice.<ref>Steadman, Ian. (22 April 2013) [https://www.wired.co.uk/news/archive/2013-04/22/yahoo-china-email Yahoo! decides to close its Chinese mail service (Wired UK)] {{webarchive |url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130831235656/http://www.wired.co.uk/news/archive/2013-04/22/yahoo-china-email |date=31 August 2013 }}. Wired.co.uk. Retrieved on 21 September 2013.</ref> In August 2013 when users were asked to transfer accounts to Alilbaba's Alimail through a note on their login page. In April it was clear that the closure was part of Yahoo's gradual exit from China since its 2005 stake acquisition in Alibaba. Users with @yahoo.com.cn or @yahoo.cn could migrate all of their data to Alimail and continue receiving emails forwarded to their Yahoo addresses until 31 December 2014. The music service that Yahoo China was offering had already shut down in January 2013.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Shu |first=Catherine |date=2013-08-19 |title=Yahoo Shuts Down Its Email Service In China |url=https://techcrunch.com/2013/08/18/yahoo-shuts-down-its-email-service-in-china/ |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20260524030644/https://techcrunch.com/2013/08/18/yahoo-shuts-down-its-email-service-in-china/ |archive-date=24 May 2026 |access-date=2026-06-04 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref> Yahoo! China closed its mail service on 19 August 2013. E-mails sent to Yahoo! China accounts could be forwarded to an Alimail box until 31 December 2014. Users were also allowed to transfer e-mail accounts to yahoo.com or any other e-mail service. It is estimated there are no more than a million users with Yahoo! Mail for China and chances are they also own other e-mail accounts.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Xiang |first=Tracey |date=19 August 2013 |title=Yahoo!China Closing Mail Service Today |work=TechNode |url=https://technode.com/2013/08/19/yahoochina-closing-mail-service-today/ |url-status=live |access-date=19 January 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200225202908/https://technode.com/2013/08/19/yahoochina-closing-mail-service-today/ |archive-date=25 February 2020 }}</ref>

=== FinTech and online payment platforms ===
[[File:Alipay payment 20160121.jpg|thumb|Food ordered with [[Alipay]]]]{{Main article|Alipay}}

In 2004, the Alibaba Group launched Alipay, a third-party [[Payment service provider|online payment platform]]. It also provides an [[escrow]] service, in which buyers can verify whether they are happy with goods they have bought before releasing money to the seller.<ref name="newschinamag1" /> Prior to Alipay, third-party payment was a legal grey area in China.<ref name=":Zhang">{{Cite book |last=Zhang |first=Angela Huyue |title=High Wire: How China Regulates Big Tech and Governs Its Economy |publisher=[[Oxford University Press]] |year=2024 |isbn=9780197682258 }}</ref>{{Rp|page=32}} As Alipay's popularity increased, it became accepted by Chinese regulators.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|pages=32-33}} In 2010, the People's Bank of China issued administrative measures regarding non-financial payment services.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=33}} These measures retroactively recognized the legal status of online third-party payment platforms like Alipay.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=33}}

Alibaba Group [[Corporate spin-off|spun off]] Alipay in 2010 in a controversial move.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Guilford |first=Gwynn |date=June 2014 |title=Alibaba has a new way of explaining its controversial Alipay spinoff |url=http://qz.com/221635/alibaba-has-a-new-way-of-explaining-its-controversial-alipay-spinoff/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150116141041/http://qz.com/221635/alibaba-has-a-new-way-of-explaining-its-controversial-alipay-spinoff/ |archive-date=16 January 2015 |access-date=8 January 2015 |website=Quartz }}</ref> In 2011, Alipay obtained a license to become one of the first licensed non-financial institutions to conduct payment operations.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=33}}

According to analyst research report, Alipay has the biggest market share in China with 300&nbsp;million users and control of just under half of China's online payment market in February 2014.<ref>{{Cite web |author=John Watling |date=14 February 2014 |title=China's Internet Giants Lead in Online Finance |url=http://www.thefinancialist.com/not-just-a-paypal-clone-chinas-internet-giants-chart-their-own-course/ |url-status=dead |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140219223010/http://www.thefinancialist.com/not-just-a-paypal-clone-chinas-internet-giants-chart-their-own-course/ |archive-date=19 February 2014 |access-date=15 February 2014 |website=The Financialist |publisher=Credit Suisse }}</ref> 

In 2013, Alipay launched a financial product platform called Yu'ebao (余额宝).<ref name=":6">{{Cite web |last=Chohan |first=Usman W. |title=Financial Innovation in China: Alibaba's Leftover Treasure – 余额宝 |url=https://www.mcgill.ca/channels/news/financial-innovation-china-alibaba%E2%80%99s-leftover-treasure-%E4%BD%99%E9%A2%9D%E5%AE%9D-234236 |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170124033714/https://www.mcgill.ca/channels/news/financial-innovation-china-alibaba%E2%80%99s-leftover-treasure-%E4%BD%99%E9%A2%9D%E5%AE%9D-234236 |archive-date=24 January 2017 |access-date=14 May 2014 |publisher=McGill University }}</ref> Alipay partnered with the [[Tianhong Asset Management]] to launch it to the general public.<ref name=":6" /> Yu'ebao offers an online money market account in which Alipay customers can deposit money and receive a higher interest rate than that available from banks.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=33}} It soon became China's largest online money market fund and prompted competitors like Baidu and Tencent to introduce alternatives.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=33}} The People's Bank of China supported the growth of such funds with a permissive regulatory environment initially but began increasing regulation of such funds in 2017.<ref name=":Zhang" />{{Rp|page=33}}

On 16 October 2014, the Alipay company was re-branded as [[Ant Financial|Ant Financial Services]] (now [[Ant Group]]).<ref>{{Cite news |date=16 October 2014 |title=Alibaba affiliate Alipay rebranded Ant in new financial services push |language=en |work=Reuters |url=https://www.reuters.com/article/us-china-alibaba-idUSKCN0I50KJ20141016 |url-status=live |access-date=16 January 2019 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180619113402/https://www.reuters.com/article/us-china-alibaba-idUSKCN0I50KJ20141016 |archive-date=19 June 2018 }}</ref>

One of the factors for Alibaba's success in this platform is the company's payment system, which can handle simultaneous transactions with credit card, [[debit card]], Alipay, [[UnionPay|Quick-pay]], and [[online banking]].{{Citation needed|date=February 2026}} Ant Financial was ranked sixth in Fortune's ''Change the World'' list, recognized for the environmental impact of its ''Ant Forest'', the world's largest platform for tracking individuals' carbon footprints.<ref>{{Cite news |last=Wang |first=Yanfei |date=9 September 2017 |title=Ant Financial ranked 6th in Fortune environmental list |work=China Daily |url=http://www.chinadaily.com.cn/business/tech/2017-09/09/content_31776061.htm |access-date=3 October 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170912165319/http://www.chinadaily.com.cn/business/tech/2017-09/09/content_31776061.htm |archive-date=12 September 2017 }}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |title=Ant Financial named in Fortune's "Change the World" list--China Economic Net |work=en.ce.cn |url=http://en.ce.cn/main/latest/201709/11/t20170911_25884165.shtml |url-status=dead |access-date=11 September 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170911204434/http://en.ce.cn/main/latest/201709/11/t20170911_25884165.shtml |archive-date=11 September 2017 }}</ref> In 2018, Ant Financial was the highest valued [[fintech]] company in the world, and the world's most valuable [[Unicorn (finance)|unicorn (start-up) company]], with a valuation of US$150&nbsp;billion.<ref>{{Cite news |title=China's
