======================================================================
ARTICLE: Payment gateway
WIKIPEDIA REVISION TEXT
======================================================================

{{Short description|Merchant service that authorizes payments}}
A '''payment gateway''' is a [[Merchant services|merchant service]] provided by an [[e-commerce]] [[application service provider]] that authorizes [[credit card]] or direct payment processing for [[ebusiness|e-businesses]], [[e-tailer|online retailers]], [[bricks and clicks business model|bricks and clicks]], or traditional [[brick and mortar]].<ref>{{cite web|title=eCommerce: Payment Gateways|url=http://www.digitalbusiness.gov.au/online-payments-and-donations-benefits-of-e-commerce/e-commerce-payment-gateways/|publisher=digitalbusiness.gov.au|access-date=20 November 2012|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20121118194457/http://www.digitalbusiness.gov.au/online-payments-and-donations-benefits-of-e-commerce/e-commerce-payment-gateways/|archive-date=18 November 2012}}</ref> The payment gateway may be provided by a bank to its customers, but can be provided by a specialised financial service provider as a separate service, such as a [[payment service provider]].

A payment gateway facilitates a payment transaction by the transfer of information between a payment portal (such as a website, [[mobile phone]] or [[interactive voice response]] service) and the front end processor or [[acquiring bank]].

Payment gateways are a service that helps merchants initiate e-commerce, in-app, and point of sale payments for a broad variety of payment methods. The gateway is not directly involved in the money flow; typically it is a web server to which a merchant's website or POS system is connected. A payment gateway often connects several
acquiring banks and payment methods under one system.

==Typical transaction processes==
When a customer orders a product from a payment gateway-enabled merchant, the payment gateway performs a variety of tasks to process the transaction.<ref>{{cite web|last=Gulati|first=Ved Prakash|title=The Empowered Internet Payment Gateway|url=http://www.iceg.net/2007/books/2/10_342_2.pdf |publisher=Computer Society of India |access-date=22 May 2013 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130810155518/http://www.iceg.net/2007/books/2/10_342_2.pdf |archive-date=10 August 2013}}</ref>{{failed verification|date=February 2024}}

# The order is placed.
# The payment gateway may allow transaction data to be sent directly from the customer's browser to the gateway, bypassing the merchant's systems, using redirect or iframe. This reduces the merchant's [[Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard|PCI DSS]] compliance obligations without redirecting the customer away from the website.{{or|date=February 2024}}
# The merchant forwards the transaction details to their payment gateway.
# The payment gateway converts the message from XML to [[ISO 8583]] or a variant message format (format understood by EFT Switches) and then forwards the transaction information to the [[payment processor]] used by the merchant's [[acquiring bank]].
# The payment processor forwards the transaction information to the [[card association]] (e.g. [[Visa Inc.|Visa]], [[Mastercard]]), which may act as the [[issuing bank]] or route the transaction to the correct card issuing bank.
# The issuing bank validates the request and sends a response back to the [[payment processor]] with a response code to indicate whether the request was approved or denied, along with the reason why the transaction failed if applicable. Meanwhile, the credit card issuer holds an authorization associated with that merchant and consumer for the approved amount.
# The payment processor forwards the response to the [[payment]] gateway, who forwards it to the website.
# The entire process typically takes 2–3 seconds.<ref>{{cite web|title=eCommerce: Choosing your payment methods|url=http://www.digitalbusiness.gov.au/e-commerce/setting-up-an-online-store/online-payment-gateways/|publisher=digitalbusiness.gov.au|access-date=19 November 2012|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130123035833/http://www.digitalbusiness.gov.au/e-commerce/setting-up-an-online-store/online-payment-gateways/|archive-date=23 January 2013}}</ref>
# The merchant then fulfills the order and the above process can be repeated but this time to "clear" the authorization by consummating (e.g. fulfilling) the transaction. This results in the issuing bank "clearing" the "auth" (i.e. moves auth-hold to a debit) and prepares them to settle with the merchant acquiring bank.
# The merchant submits all their approved authorizations, in a "batch" at the end of the day, to their [[acquiring bank]] for settlement via its processor. This typically reduces or "clears" the corresponding "auth" if it has not been explicitly "cleared".
# The acquiring bank makes the batch settlement request of the credit card issuer.
# The credit card issuer makes a settlement payment to the acquiring bank (the next day in most cases).
# The [[acquiring bank]] subsequently deposits the total of the approved funds into the merchant's nominated account (the same day or next day). This could be an account with the acquiring bank if the merchant does their banking with the same bank, or an account with another bank.
# The entire process from authorization to settlement to funding typically takes 2-3 days.

Many payment gateways also provide tools to automatically screen orders for fraud and calculate tax in real time prior to the authorization request being sent to the processor. Tools to detect fraud include [[geolocation]], velocity pattern analysis, [[OFAC list]] lookups, 'deny-list' lookups, delivery address verification, computer finger printing technology, identity morphing detection, and basic [[Address Verification System|AVS]] checks.

== White label payment gateway ==
Some payment gateways offer white label services, which allow [[payment service provider]]s, e-commerce platforms, ISOs, resellers, or acquiring banks to fully brand the payment gateway’s technology as their own.<ref>{{Cite news|url=http://www.investopedia.com/terms/w/white-label-product.asp|title=White Label Product|author=Investopedia Staff|date=2008-05-21|work=Investopedia|access-date=2017-07-20|language=en-US}}</ref> This means [[Payment service provider|PSPs]] or other third parties can own the end-to-end user experience without bringing payments operations—and additional risk management and compliance responsibility—in house, although the party offering the white labelled solution to its customers might still be responsible for some regulatory requirements such as [[Know your customer]].<ref>{{Cite web|url=http://www.mastercard.com/gateway/white_label/mpgs-acquirer-services.html|title=Acquirer Services - White Label Payment Processing - MasterCard Payment Gateway Services|website=www.mastercard.com|access-date=2017-07-20 }}</ref>

* [[List of online payment service providers]]

======================================================================
ARTICLE: Reverse logistics
WIKIPEDIA REVISION TEXT
======================================================================

{{Short description|All operations related to the reuse of products and materials}}
{{Multiple issues|
{{Tone|date=June 2023}}
{{More citations needed|date=June 2023}}
}}
{{research|date=June 2023}}
'''Reverse logistics''' encompasses all operations related to the upstream movement of products and materials. It is "''the process of moving [[good (economics)|goods]] from their typical final destination for the purpose of capturing value, or proper disposal. [[Remanufacturing]] and [[Refurbishment (electronics)|refurbishing]] activities also may be included in the definition of reverse logistics''".<ref>{{Cite web |title=Editions |url=https://rla.org/media/magazine/recent |access-date=2025-05-07 |website=rla.org}}</ref> Environmental concerns and the development of green supply chain management practices have increased the relevance of reverse logistics.<ref>Srivastava, Samir K. "Network Design for Reverse Logistics", ''Omega'', '''2008''', 36(4), 535-548.</ref>

Academic and professional interest in reverse logistics has grown considerably in recent decades. The first use of the term "reverse logistics" in a publication was by James R. Stock in a white paper titled ''Reverse Logistics'', published by the Council of Logistics Management in 1992.<ref>James R. Stock, Reverse Logistics (Oak Brook, IL:  Council of Logistics Management, 1992)</ref>  The concept was further refined in subsequent publications by Stock (1998) in another Council of Logistics Management book, titled ''Development and Implementation of Reverse Logistics Programs'',<ref>James R. Stock, Development and Implementation of Reverse Logistics Programs (Oak Brook, IL:  Council of Logistics Management, 1998)</ref> and by Rogers and Tibben-Lembke (1999) in a book published by the Reverse Logistics Association titled ''Going Backwards:  Reverse Logistics Trends and Practices''.<ref>Dale S. Rogers and Ronald S. Tibben-Lembke, Going Backwards:  Reverse Logistics Trends and Practices (Reno, NV:  Reverse Logistics Executive Council, 1999)</ref> Rogers and Tibben-Lembke focused their work on the idea that reverse logistics was not only about green and environmental issues as Stock and most of the European reverse logistics researchers were thinking at the time, but rather that utilizing reverse logistics as a tool to reclaim value from distressed assets was most important. The idea that reverse logistics could be profitable was at the time a new idea.

The reverse logistics process includes the management and the sale of surplus items, as well as returned equipment and machines, particularly from the hardware leasing business. Traditional logistics typically involves the forward movement of goods toward the customer, whereas reverse logistics refers to the backward flow of goods in the supply chain. In such cases, resources move at least one step back in the supply chain — for example, from the customer to the distributor or manufacturer.<ref>Rengel, P. & Seydl, C. (May 2002). [http://www.seydl.at/papers/Rengel_Seydl_Reverse_Logisitics_2002_23_05_en.pdf Completing the Supply Chain Model] at seydl.at. Retrieved on 2019-07-28.</ref>

As of 2023, the global reverse logistics market is estimated to be worth approximately $993.28 billion. This value is projected to increase at a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 10.34% from 2023 to 2032.<ref name=":1">{{Cite press release |title=Topic: Reverse logistics |url=https://www.globenewswire.com/en/news-release/2023/04/11/2644681/28124/en/Global-Reverse-Logistics-Market-Report-2023-Increase-in-E-Commerce-Activities-and-Worldwide-Trade-Drives-Growth.html |access-date=2023-06-09 |website=GlobalNewsWire |date=11 April 2023 |language=en}}</ref>

An important idea in the reverse logistics space is the development and usage of secondary markets. Rogers, Z. S., Davletshin, M., Rogers, D. S., Chen, H., Korde, R. Y., & Greve, C. (2025). Unveiling the Structure of Reverse Supply Networks: An Empirical Exploration. Journal of Business Logistics, 46(3), e70014. https://doi.org/10.1111/jbl.70014 showed that secondary markets in the U.S. amount to around 3% of GDP for the last several years. Items returned either because of consumer regret or overstocks have become an important source of supply for a large part of U.S. retailers.

== Business implications ==
In current marketplace, many retailers handle merchandise returns as isolated transactions.<ref>{{Cite web |title=The benefits of efficient reverse logistics |url=https://www.airpharmlogistics.com/en/reverse-logistics-efficient-benefits/ |access-date=2022-10-31 |publisher=Airpharm}}</ref> A significant challenge for retailers and vendors is to manage returns efficiently, ensuring the quick, accurate, and cost-effective collection and reintegration of merchandise. As customer expectations for precision and speed in returns processing continue to rise, logistics companies are increasingly responsible for minimizing the time between return initiation and resale.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Editions |url=https://rla.org/media/magazine/recent |access-date=2025-05-13 |website=rla.org}}</ref> By implementing best practices in returns management, retailers can optimize operational efficiency while addressing customer satisfaction and retention concerns.<ref>Greer, 2004</ref> Due to its strong link to customer retention, reverse logistics has become a key component of Service Lifecycle Management (SLM). SLM is a strategic business approach focused on enhancing customer loyalty by integrating and coordinating service-related data and processes to improve overall operational efficiency. [https://www.techtarget.com/searcherp/definition/service-lifecycle-management-SLM?utm_source=chatgpt.com]

Reverse logistics extends beyond returns management and encompasses “activities related to returns avoidance, gatekeeping, disposal, and all other [[Aftermarket (merchandise)|after-market]] supply chain issues.<ref>Rogers, 2002</ref>

Returns management, increasingly recognized for its influence on competitive positioning, serves as a vital connection between marketing and logistics. Its cross-functional nature implies that firms can benefit significantly from enhancing internal integration. In particular, the ability to respond to and plan for external influences on the returns management process is improved through effective internal coordination.<ref>{{citation |url=http://www.dea.univr.it/jsp/visualizzaAllegato.jsp?id=26331&mime=application%2Fpdf&filename=Russo+Supply+chain+strategy+IJPDLM.pdf&localname=allegatooa_26331.pdf |title=The returns management process in supply chain strategy |last1=Mollenkopf |first1=D. |last2=Russo |first2=I. |first3=R. |last3=Frankel |journal=International Journal of Physical Distribution & Logistics Management |volume=37 |issue=7 |year=2007 |pages=568–92 |access-date=2008-05-05 |doi=10.1108/09600030710776482}}</ref> A key consideration in a firm's returns planning is the residual value of the returned material and strategies to recover that value.<ref>Srivastava, Samir K. "Value Recovery Network Design for Product Returns", ''International Journal of Physical Distribution and Logistics Management'', '''2008''', 38(4), 311-331.</ref> Returned products, or components, may also be redirected to suppliers or other supply chain partners for remanufacturing.<ref>Madaan, J. & Wadhwa, S. (2007) ''Flexible Process Planning Approaches for Sustainable Decisions in Reverse Logistics System'', Global Journal of Flexible Systems Management. Vol. 8, No. 4. p. 1-8</ref>

Implementing reverse logistics, like other supply chain operations, involves inherent risks. Despite its growing relevance, research on the specific risks associated with reverse logistics operations remains limited. Panjehfouladgaran and Lim (2020) addressed this gap by introducing the concept of Reverse Logistics Risk Management (RLRM), proposing structured approaches to mitigate these challenges.<ref>{{Cite journal |last1=Panjehfouladgaran |first1=Hamidreza |last2=Lim |first2=Stanley Frederick W.T. |date=2020 |title=Reverse logistics risk management: identification, clustering and risk mitigation strategies |url=https://www.emerald.com/insight/content/doi/10.1108/MD-01-2018-0010/full/html |journal=Management Decision |volume=58 |issue=7 |pages=1449–1474 |doi=10.1108/MD-01-2018-0010 |url-access=subscription }}</ref> According to industry data, return costs can account for up to 7% of an enterprise's gross sales, a significant expense for many businesses.[https://tacticallogistic.com/logistics/3pl-return-process-benefits/?utm_source=chatgpt.com][[Third-party logistics]] who often manage these returns, typically customize contracts to suit the size and operational needs of their clients. These providers generally realize profit margins between 12% and 15% on reverse logistics services. Return rates also vary by channel: approximately 8–10% of in-store (brick-and-mortar) purchases are returned, compared to about 20% of online ([[E-commerce]]) purchases. In the United States alone, return deliveries were projected to cost $550 billion in 2020.[https://www.ascm.org/topics/reverse-logistics/] December is traditionally the busiest month for reverse logistics in the United States, with UPS processing over 1 million returned packages daily through Christmas.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Number Of Packages Returned Expected To Peak Before Christmas, Reflecting E-Commerce Shift |url=https://pressroom.ups.com/pressroom/ContentDetailsViewer.page?ConceptType=PressReleases&id=1545149633056-650 |access-date=2020-08-28 |publisher=UPS Pressroom}}</ref>

Research on reverse logistics indicates that 84.6% of companies in the United States use the secondary market, with 70% viewing it as a competitive advantage."<ref>{{cite web |last1=Reverse Logistics Sustainability Council |url=http://www.reverselogistics.com/ |title=Secondary Market Research |access-date=9 August 2018}}</ref> A study conducted in Taiwan identified three primary factors influencing the adoption of reverse logistics in businesses: economic, environmental, and social needs.The research, which surveyed 12 environmental management experts from Taiwanese electronics firms, found that economic needs were the most influential, with an importance weight of <u>0.4842.</u> Environmental needs followed with a weight of <u>0.3728</u>, while social needs were considered less significant, with a weight of <u>0.1430</u>.<ref name=":0">{{Cite journal |last1=Chiou |first1=Cherng Ying |last2=Chen |first2=Hui Chiu |last3=Yu |first3=Cheng Tao |last4=Yeh |first4=Chun Yuan |date=2012-01-01 |title=Consideration Factors of Reverse Logistics Implementation: A Case Study of Taiwan's Electronics Industry |journal=Procedia - Social and Behavioral Sciences |series=ASIA PACIFIC BUSINESS INNOVATION AND TECHNOLOGY MANAGEMENT SOCIETY |volume=40 |pages=375–381 |doi=10.1016/j.sbspro.2012.03.203 |issn=1877-0428 |doi-access=free}}</ref>

The economic motivation for reverse logistics in the United States is often driven by the potential to recover value from returned goods. In contrast, a study from Taiwan suggests that the significance of environmental concerns is influenced by global waste management practices, particularly in developed regions such as the European Union, Japan, and the United States. For instance, the European Union's Waste Electrical and Electronic Equipment (WEEE) Directive holds producers responsible for the collection, treatment, recycling, and recovery of electrical and electronic waste.<ref name=":0"/>

==Return of unsold goods==
In certain [[Industry (economics)|industries]], particularly those dealing with perishable or time-sensitive products such as newspapers and magazines, goods are often supplied to downstream members of the supply chain with the agreement that unsold items may be returned for credit. This arrangement serves as an incentive for retailers and distributors to stock larger quantities, as the risk of unsold inventory is assumed by upstream suppliers. However, this practice also introduces specific risks. Downstream partners may overorder intentionally, knowing they can return unsold goods, thereby enhancing their service levels without bearing the associated inventory risks. In effect, suppliers absorb the financial burden of excess stock. Consequently, it is crucial for suppliers to monitor customer accounts carefully to identify and account for any hidden costs that may arise from such return agreements.<ref>{{Cite journal |last=van Hoek |first=Remko |last2=Johnson |first2=Mark |date=2010-02-02 |editor-last=Halldórsson |editor-first=Árni |title=Sustainability and energy efficiency: Research implications from an academic roundtable and two case examples |url=https://www.emerald.com/insight/content/doi/10.1108/09600031011018064/full/html |journal=International Journal of Physical Distribution & Logistics Management |language=en |volume=40 |issue=1/2 |pages=148–158 |doi=10.1108/09600031011018064 |issn=0960-0035|url-access=subscription }}</ref>

==Reusable packaging==
[[File:Woda.mineralna.w.skrzynkach.jpg|thumb|upright|Reusable [[mineral water]] bottles in crates]]
[[Reusable packaging]] systems require a [[Circular economy|closed-loop]] logistics system. Examples include reusable [[pallet]]s, [[bulk box]]es such as [[Euro container]]s, [[reuse of bottles|reusable bottles]] for milk, soda, and  beer, compressed gas cylinders, beer kegs.<ref>{{cite journal
 | last =Zimmerman
 | first =T
 | title =Single-use vs. reusable packaging in e-commerce: comparing carbon footprints and identifying break-even points
 | journal =GAIA
 | volume =29
 | issue =3
 | pages =176–173
 | date =2020
| doi =10.14512/gaia.29.3.8
 | s2cid =235861717

| access-date = 7 May 2023
}}</ref>

==Refusal of the products in the cash on delivery (COD)==
In [[e-commerce]], many websites offer the option of [[cash on delivery]] (COD) to customers. Occasionally, customers refuse to accept the product at the time of delivery since there is no prior commitment to take it. Then the logistics service provider follows the process of reverse logistics on the refused cargo. When this occurs, the logistics service provider initiates the reverse logistics process for the refused shipment, commonly known as Return to Origin (RTO). During this process, the e-commerce company returns the refused goods to its inventory after conducting quality checks according to the company's standards.<ref>{{cite book| title= Business Logistics & Management - Theory and Practice |first1= J J |last1= Vogt| first2=W J |last2= Pienaar |first3=P W C |last3= de Wit | publisher= OXFORD |year= 2002 |page= 236 }}</ref>

'''Shopify Inc.''' is a Canadian multinational [[e-commerce]] company headquartered in [[Ottawa]], Ontario, that operates a cloud e-commerce management platform (CEMP) for [[retail]] [[Point of sale|point-of-sale]] systems. The company has over five million customers and processed US$292.3 billion in transactions in 2024, of which 57% was in the [[United States]].<ref name="10K">{{cite web | url=https://www.sec.gov/ix?doc=/Archives/edgar/data/0001594805/000159480525000012/shop-20241231.htm | title=Shopify Inc. 2024 Form 10-K Annual Report | publisher=[[U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission]] | date=February 11, 2025}}</ref> Major customers include [[Tesla, Inc.|Tesla]], [[LVMH]], [[Nestlé]], [[PepsiCo]], [[AB InBev]], [[Kraft Heinz]], [[Lindt]], [[Whole Foods Market]], [[Red Bull]], and [[Hyatt]].<ref>{{Cite news | url=https://finance.yahoo.com/news/15-biggest-companies-shopify-170757960.html | title=15 Biggest Companies That Use Shopify | first=Trish | last=Novicio | via=[[Yahoo Finance]] | date=April 8, 2021}}</ref>

The company's software has been praised for its ease of use and reasonable fee structure.<ref>{{Cite news | url=https://www.networksolutions.com/blog/pros-cons-shopify/ | title=Shopify Pros and Cons | work=[[Network Solutions]]}}</ref> It has been described as the "go-to e-commerce platform for startups".<ref name=goto/>  

== History ==
===2006: Founding===
Shopify was founded in 2006 by friends [[Tobias Lütke]], Daniel Weinand and Scott Lake after launching Snowdevil, an online store for [[snowboarding]] equipment, in 2004.<ref>{{cite news | url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/report-on-business/small-business/sb-growth/fluke-and-luck-shopifys-co-founder-profited-from-both/article4575360/?page=all |title=Fluke and luck: Shopify's co-founder profited from both | first=DIANE | last=JERMYN | work=[[The Globe and Mail]] | date=October 5, 2012}}</ref> Dissatisfied with the existing e-commerce products on the market, Lütke, a [[computer programmer]] by trade, instead built his own.<ref>{{cite news |url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/report-on-business/rob-magazine/meet-our-ceo-of-the-year/article21734931/ |title=Our Canadian CEO of the year you've probably never heard of | last=Cole | first=Trevor | work=[[The Globe and Mail]] |date=November 27, 2014 |archive-date=May 4, 2020 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200504010740/https://www.theglobeandmail.com/report-on-business/rob-magazine/meet-our-ceo-of-the-year/article21734931/ |url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news | last=Whittaker | first=Stephanie | url=https://www.sitesellinc.com/gazette-web-safety-net.html | title=The Web as a safety net | work=[[The Montreal Gazette]] | date=May 4, 2009 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210506211455/https://www.sitesellinc.com/gazette-web-safety-net.html |archive-date=May 6, 2021 | url-status=live}}</ref>

Lütke used the [[open source]] web application framework [[Ruby on Rails]] to build Snowdevil's online store and launched it after two months of development.<ref name=glass>{{Cite web | url=https://htmlstream.com/unify/portfolio-case-study.html | title=UV Violet glass | work=HTML Stream}}</ref><ref name=raises7m>{{Cite news | url=https://allthingsd.com/20101213/e-commerce-assistant-shopify-raises-7-million-in-first-round/ | last=Duryee | first=Tricia | title=E-Commerce Assistant Shopify Raises $8 Million in First Round | work=[[All Things Digital]] | date=December 13, 2010 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200807050059/http://emoney.allthingsd.com/20101213/e-commerce-assistant-shopify-raises-7-million-in-first-round/ | archive-date=August 7, 2020 | url-status=live}}</ref> The Snowdevil founders launched the platform as Shopify in June 2006.<ref name=glass/> Shopify created an open-source template language called Liquid, which is written in [[Ruby (programming language)|Ruby]] and has been used since 2006.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://shopify.github.io/liquid/|title=Liquid template language |website=Liquid template language|access-date=October 7, 2021}}</ref>

In June 2009, Shopify launched an [[application programming interface]] (API) platform and App Store.<ref>{{Cite web | url=https://htmlstream.com/space/portfolio-case-study.html | title=Transforming the online E-commerce company Shopify into a success. | work=HTML Stream}}</ref> The API allows developers to create applications for Shopify online stores and then sell them on the Shopify App Store.<ref name=goto>{{cite magazine | url=https://www.entrepreneur.com/science-technology/how-shopify-became-the-go-to-ecommerce-platform-for-startups/222967 | title=How Shopify Became the Go-To E-commerce Platform for Startups | date=March 12, 2012 | last=Duncan | first=Katherine | magazine=[[Entrepreneur (magazine)|Entrepreneur]] | archive-date=February 28, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150228183452/http://www.entrepreneur.com/article/222967 | url-status=live}}</ref>

===2010s===
In January 2010, Shopify started its Build-A-Business competition, in which participants create a business using its commerce platform.<ref>{{Cite news | last=Kolodny | first=Lora | url=https://archive.nytimes.com/boss.blogs.nytimes.com/2010/07/19/dodocase-top-shop/ | title=Maker of iPad Cases Wins Shopify Competition | work=[[The New York Times]] | date=July 19, 2010 |  archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220607074645/https://boss.blogs.nytimes.com/2010/07/19/dodocase-top-shop/ | archive-date=June 7, 2022 | url-status=live}}</ref><ref name=teamsup>{{Cite news | first=Rip | last=Empson | work=[[TechCrunch]] | date=July 10, 2012 | url=https://techcrunch.com/2012/07/10/shopify-build-a-business-competition/ | title=Shopify Teams Up With Tim Ferriss, Eric Ries, FUBU Founder To Help You Build A $1M eCommerce Biz | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170707223610/https://techcrunch.com/2012/07/10/shopify-build-a-business-competition/ |archive-date=July 7, 2017 | url-status=live}}</ref> The winners of the competition received cash prizes and mentorship from entrepreneurs, such as [[Richard Branson]], [[Eric Ries]] and others.<ref name=teamsup/> In April of that year, Shopify launched a free [[mobile app]] on the [[Apple Inc.|Apple]] [[App Store (iOS)|App Store]]. The app allows Shopify store owners to view and manage their stores from [[IOS (Apple)|iOS]] mobile devices.<ref name=Redesign>{{cite news | first=Rip | last=Empson | work=[[TechCrunch]] |date=April 11, 2013 |title=After 7 Years & 50K Storefronts Created, Shopify Launches Major Redesign To Simplify Online Store-Building |url=https://techcrunch.com/2013/04/11/after-7-years-50k-storefronts-launched-shopify-launches-major-redesign-to-simplify-online-store-building/  |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170807120923/https://techcrunch.com/2013/04/11/after-7-years-50k-storefronts-launched-shopify-launches-major-redesign-to-simplify-online-store-building/ |archive-date=August 7, 2017 |url-status=live}}</ref>

In December 2010, Shopify raised $7 million from a [[series A round]] from [[Bessemer Venture Partners]], [[FirstMark Capital]], and Felicis Ventures at a $20 million pre-money valuation.<ref name=raises7m/> At that time, the company had an annualized transaction value of $132 million.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify |url=https://www.bvp.com/memos/shopify |website=[[Bessemer Venture Partners]]}}</ref> In October 2011, it raised $15 million in a Series B round.<ref>{{cite news |url=https://allthingsd.com/20111017/shopifys-picks-up-15-million-as-it-faces-new-competition-from-ebay/  |last=Duryee |first=Tricia |title=Shopify Picks Up $15 Million as It Faces New Competition From eBay | work=[[All Things Digital]] |date=October 17, 2011 |archive-date=October 7, 2015|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151007152906/http://allthingsd.com/20111017/shopifys-picks-up-15-million-as-it-faces-new-competition-from-ebay/ |url-status=live}}</ref>

In August 2013, Shopify launched Shopify Payments in partnership with [[Stripe, Inc.|Stripe]]. Shopify Payments allows merchants to accept payments without requiring a third-party payment gateway.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Etherington |first=Darrell |date=2013-08-12 |title=Shopify Debuts Fully Integrated Credit Card Payment Processing For Its E-Commerce Platform |url=https://techcrunch.com/2013/08/12/shopify-debuts-fully-integrated-credit-card-payment-processing-for-its-e-commerce-platform/ |access-date=2023-01-22 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US |archive-date=June 22, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220622053022/https://techcrunch.com/2013/08/12/shopify-debuts-fully-integrated-credit-card-payment-processing-for-its-e-commerce-platform/ |url-status=live }}</ref> The company also announced the launch of a [[point of sale]] system to enable in-person sales in addition to online.<ref>{{cite web | url=https://techcrunch.com/2013/08/28/shopify-launches-point-of-sale-system-to-unify-online-and-brick-and-mortar-retail/ | title=Shopify Launches Point-Of-Sale System to Unify Online and Brick-And-Mortar Retail | date=August 28, 2013 }}</ref> The company received $100 million in Series C funding in December 2013.<ref name="New York Times">{{cite web |last=Etherington |first=Darrell|title=Shopify Raises $100M To Drop The 'E' And Become The Commerce Company That Spans On- And Offline |date=December 12, 2013 |url=https://techcrunch.com/2013/12/11/shopify-100-million-series-c/ |access-date=December 13, 2013|archive-date=January 15, 2014 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140115014141/http://techcrunch.com/2013/12/11/shopify-100-million-series-c/|url-status=live}}</ref> Shopify earned $105 million in revenue in 2014, twice as much as it raised the previous year.<ref name="vb">{{cite web |url=https://venturebeat.com/2015/05/21/shopify-pops-65-on-the-nyse-starts-trading-at-28-per-share/ |title=Shopify pops 65% on the NYSE, starts trading above $28 per share |last1=Reader |first1=Ruth |date=May 21, 2015 |publisher=VentureBeat |access-date=August 20, 2015 |archive-date=September 19, 2015 In April 2016, Shopify announced Shopify Capital, a cash advance product. Shopify Capital was initially piloted to merchants within the US and allowed merchants to receive an advance on future earnings processed through its payment gateway.<ref>{{cite web | url=https://betakit.com/shopify-launches-shopify-capital-to-fund-entrepreneurs-on-its-platform/ | title=Shopify launches Shopify Capital to fund entrepreneurs on its platform &#124; BetaKit | date=April 27, 2016 }}</ref> Since its launch in 2016, Shopify Capital has provided more than $5.1 billion in funding to Shopify merchants, with a maximum advance of $2 million.<ref>{{cite web | url=https://thelogic.co/news/shopifys-loan-business-is-booming/ | title=Shopify's loan business is booming | date=December 19, 2023 }}</ref> On June 7, 2016, Shopify launched its Shopify Plus Partners Program, to help agencies connect with evolving businesses in ecommerce space.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify Launches Shopify Plus Partner Program |url=https://www.shopify.com/news/shopify-launches-shopify-plus-partner-program |language=en}}</ref> On October 3, 2016, Shopify acquired Boltmade.<ref>{{cite web|title =Shopify acquires product design firm Boltmade to boost Shopify Plus|url 2016, Shopify partnered with Paystack which allowed Nigerian online retailers to accept payments from customers around the world.<ref>{{Cite In January 2017, Shopify announced integration with Amazon that would allow merchants to sell on Amazon from their Shopify stores.<ref name=":0">{{Cite web |url=http://fortune.com/2017/03/15/shopify-ecommerce-revolution/ |title=Shopify's E-commerce Revolution |website=Fortune|access-date=April 24, 2017|archive-date=May 19, 2017 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170519043054/http://fortune.com/2017/03/15/shopify-ecommerce-revolution/ |url-status=live}}</ref> In April 2017, Shopify introduced its Chip & Swipe Reader, a [[Bluetooth]] enabled debit and credit card reader for [[brick and mortar]] retail purchases.<ref>{{Cite news |url=http://fortune.com/2017/04/20/shopify-credit-card-reader/|title=Shopify Takes on Square With New Credit Card Reader|last=Rao|first=Leena|date=April 20, 2017|work=[[Fortune (magazine)|Fortune]]|access-date=June 4, 2017 |archive-date=June 1, 2017|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170601201848/http://fortune.com/2017/04/20/shopify-credit-card-reader/|url-status=live}}</ref> The company has since released additional technology for brick-and-mortar retailers, including a point-of-sale system with a Dock and Retail Stand similar to that offered by Square, and a tappable chip card reader.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2019/04/25/shopify-retail-hardwre-colelction/|title=Shopify unveils a new lineup of retail hardware|website=TechCrunch|date=April 25, 2019 |language=en-US|access-date=May 10, 2019|archive-date=August 19, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220819061034/https://techcrunch.com/2019/04/25/shopify-retail-hardwre-colelction/|url-status=live}}</ref>

Shopify announced a one-click accelerated checkout feature called Shopify Pay in April 2017 as an exclusive feature for merchants using Shopify Payments as their payment processor.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Etherington |first=Darrell |date=2017-04-20 |title=Shopify Pay aims to complete the picture for online checkout |url=https://techcrunch.com/2017/04/20/shopify-pay-aims-to-complete-the-picture-for-online-checkout/ |access-date=2023-01-22 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US |archive-date=January 22, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230122183507/https://techcrunch.com/2017/04/20/shopify-pay-aims-to-complete-the-picture-for-online-checkout/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Customers can save their shipping and payment information for future purchases from all participating Shopify stores. In November 2017, Shopify announced Arrive, a mobile application to help customers track packages from both Shopify merchants and other e-commerce websites.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Etherington |first=Darrell |date=2017-11-29 |title=Shopify's new Arrive app is your package tracking pal |url=https://techcrunch.com/2017/11/29/shopifys-new-arrive-app-is-your-package-tracking-pal/ |access-date=2023-01-22 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US |archive-date=January 22, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230122183505/https://techcrunch.com/2017/11/29/shopifys-new-arrive-app-is-your-package-tracking-pal/ |url-status=live }}</ref>

In September 2018, Shopify announced plans to expand its office space in [[Toronto]]'s King West neighborhood in 2022 as part of "The Well" complex, jointly owned by Allied Properties REIT and RioCan REIT.<ref>{{cite news |last1=O'Kane |first1=Josh |title=Shopify will invest up to $500-million in new Toronto office |url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/business/technology/article-shopify-will-invest-up-to-500-million-in-new-toronto-office/ |website=The Globe and Mail |date=September 27, 2018 |access-date=October 2, 2018 |archive-date=October 2, 2018 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181002065324/https://www.theglobeandmail.com/business/technology/article-shopify-will-invest-up-to-500-million-in-new-toronto-office/ |url-status=live }}</ref> In October 2018, Shopify opened its first flagship, a physical space for business owners in [[Los Angeles]].<ref name=":4">{{Cite web |url=https://www.bizjournals.com/losangeles/news/2018/10/11/shopify-opens-its-first-physical-location.html |title=Shopify goes to L.A. to open its first physical location, seeking to help businesses grow|last=Ellingson |first=Annlee |date=October 11, 2018|website=LA Biz|access-date=April 15, 2020|archive-date=May 13, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210513230914/https://www.bizjournals.com/losangeles/news/2018/10/11/shopify-opens-its-first-physical-location.html|url-status=live}}</ref> The space offered educational classes, coworking space, a "genius bar" for companies that use Shopify software, and workshops.<ref name=":4" /> Online cannabis sales in Ontario, Canada, used Shopify's software when the drug was [[cannabis in Canada|legalized]] in October 2018.  Shopify's software is also used for in-person cannabis sales in Ontario since becoming legal in 2019.<ref>{{cite news | date=February 12, 2018 | title=Ontario Chooses Shopify to Run Online Cannabis Sales | url=https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/ottawa/ontario-shopify-marijuana-online-shopping-1.4531228 | work=CBC | access-date=October 18, 2018 | archive-date=October 2, 2018 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20181002062414/https://www.cbc.ca/news/canada/ottawa/ontario-shopify-marijuana-online-shopping-1.4531228 | url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | date=October 17, 2018 | title=Pot is Now Legal in Ontario. In January 2019, Shopify announced the launch of Shopify Studios, a full-service television and film content and production house.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.bizjournals.com/losangeles/news/2019/01/24/shopify-launches-tv-and-film-production-studio.html|title=Shopify launches TV and film production studio focused on entrepreneurs|last=Chen|first=I-Chun|date=January 24, 2019|access-date=January 24, 2019|archive-date=October 29, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20201029232849/https://www.bizjournals.com/losangeles/news/2019/01/24/shopify-launches-tv-and-film-production-studio.html|url-status=live}}</ref> On March 22, 2019, Shopify and email marketing platform [[Mailchimp]] ended an integration agreement over disputes involving customer privacy and data collection.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2019/03/22/mailchimp-and-shopify-break-up/|title=Mailchimp and Shopify break up|website=TechCrunch|date=March 22, 2019 |language=en-US|access-date=May 29, 2019|archive-date=August 19, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220819061033/https://techcrunch.com/2019/03/22/mailchimp-and-shopify-break-up/|url-status=live}}</ref> In April 2019, Shopify announced an integration with [[Snapchat]] to allow Shopify merchants to buy and manage Snapchat Story ads directly on the Shopify platform. The company had previously secured similar integration partnerships with Facebook and Google.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.cnbc.com/2019/04/29/snap-shopify-partner-for-small-business-advertising-tool.html|title=Snap, Shopify partner for small business advertising tool|last=Graham|first=Megan|date=April 29, 2019|website=www.cnbc.com|access-date=May 10, 2019|archive-date=May 10, 2019|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190510150544/https://www.cnbc.com/2019/04/29/snap-shopify-partner-for-small-business-advertising-tool.html|url-status=live}}</ref> On August 14, 2019, Shopify launched Shopify Chat, a new native chat function that allows merchants to have real-time conversations with customers visiting Shopify stores online.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.zdnet.com/article/shopify-adds-native-chat-function-to-ping-app/|title=Shopify adds native chat function to Ping app|date=August 14, 2019|website=[[Zdnet]]|language=en-CA|access-date=August 22, 2019|archive-date=June 6, 2020|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200606211856/https://www.zdnet.com/article/shopify-adds-native-chat-function-to-ping-app/|url-status=live}}</ref>

On January 18, 2022, Shopify announced a partnership with [[JD.com]] to let U.S. merchants expand their operations in China, listing their products on JD's cross-border e-commerce platform JD channels.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify launches new 'Linkpop' link in bio tool with built-in e-commerce features In June 2022, Shopify partnered with [[Twitter]]. As a part of the deal, Twitter announced that it would launch a sales channel app for all of Shopify's U.S. merchants through its app store.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Twitter partners with Shopify to bring merchants' products to Twitter Shopping |url=https://techcrunch.com/2022/06/22/twitter-partners-with-shopify-to-bring-merchants-products-to-twitter-shopping/ |access-date=2022-06-23 |website=TechCrunch |date=June 22, 2022 |language=en-US |archive-date=June 29, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220629141808/https://techcrunch.com/2022/06/22/twitter-partners-with-shopify-to-bring-merchants-products-to-twitter-shopping/ |url-status=live }}</ref> Shopify also partnered with [[PayPal]] to offer Shopify Payments to merchants in France.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2022-06-28 |title=Shopify s'allie à PayPal pour lancer sa propre solution de paiement |url=https://www.lesechos.fr/finance-marches/banque-assurances/shopify-sallie-a-paypal-pour-lancer-sa-propre-solution-de-paiement-1582301 |access-date=2023-03-10 |website=[[Les Echos (France)|Les Echos]] |language=fr |archive-date=March 10, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230310000407/https://www.lesechos.fr/finance-marches/banque-assurances/shopify-sallie-a-paypal-pour-lancer-sa-propre-solution-de-paiement-1582301 |url-status=live }}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify Payments Terms of Service – France |url=https://www.shopify.com/legal/terms-payments-fr |access-date=2023-03-10 |website=Shopify |language=en |archive-date=March 10, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230310000410/https://www.shopify.com/legal/terms-payments-fr |url-status=live }}</ref> On July 26, 2022, Lütke announced immediate [[layoffs]] totalling roughly 10 percent of its workforce. In a message to employees, the CEO and founder said the company's planning on growth rates continuing on the trajectory of the past two years "didn't pay off" and forced the company to downsize.<ref>{{cite news |last=Thorbecke |first=Catherine |url=https://www.cnn.com/2022/07/26/tech/shopify-layoffs/index.html |title=Shopify to cut 10% of staff after making 'wrong' bet about pandemic online shopping boom |work=[[CNN]] |date=2022-07-26 |access-date=2022-07-26 |archive-date=July 26, 2022 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220726200430/https://www.cnn.com/2022/07/26/tech/shopify-layoffs/index.html |url-status=live }}</ref> In August 2022, Shopify announced it was making e-commerce marketing automation platform, [[Klaviyo]], the recommended email solution partner for its Shopify Plus merchant platform, with a US$100{{nbsp}}million strategic investment into the company.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20220802005457/en/Klaviyo-and-Shopify-Announce-Strategic-Partnership|title=Klaviyo and Shopify Announce Strategic Partnership|website=Business Wire|date=2 August 2022|language=en|access-date=2022-08-02|archive-date=August 3, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220803102645/https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20220802005457/en/Klaviyo-and-Shopify-Announce-Strategic-Partnership|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2022/08/02/shopify-makes-100m-strategic-investment-in-marketing-automation-startup-klaviyo/|title=Shopify makes $100M strategic investment in marketing automation startup Klaviyo|website=Techcrunch|date=2 August 2022|language=en|access-date=2022-08-02|archive-date=August 3, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220803102436/https://techcrunch.com/2022/08/02/shopify-makes-100m-strategic-investment-in-marketing-automation-startup-klaviyo/|url-status=live}}</ref>

In May 2023, Shopify laid off approximately 20% of its workforce and sold Shopify Logistics, its in-house logistics arm, to [[Flexport]], which subsequently became the preferred logistics partner for the e-commerce platform.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://news.shopify.com/important-team-and-business-changes|title=Important Team and Business Changes|website=Shopify News|date=13 May 2023|language=en|access-date=2023-05-13|archive-date=May 14, 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230514000000/https://news.shopify.com/important-team-and-business-changes|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2023/05/04/how-shopify-bungled-its-latest-layoffs-and-made-employees-feel-like-npcs/|title=How Shopify Bungled Its Latest Layoffs and Made Employees Feel Like NPCs|website=TechCrunch|date=4 May 2023|language=en|access-date=2023-05-13|archive-date=May 14, 2023|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230514000100/https://techcrunch.com/2023/05/04/how-shopify-bungled-its-latest-layoffs-and-made-employees-feel-like-npcs/|url-status=live}}</ref>

On March 18, 2025, Shopify announced that it will transfer its U.S. listing from the NYSE to the [[Nasdaq Global Select Market]]. Shopify began trading as a Nasdaq-listed security on March 31,<ref>{{cite web | url=https://techcrunch.com/2025/03/19/shopify-transfers-its-us-listing-from-the-nyse-to-the-nasdaq/ | title=Shopify transfers its US listing from the NYSE to the Nasdaq | date=March 19, 2025 }}</ref> and became a component of the [[Nasdaq-100]] index on May 19 at the stock market open.<ref>{{cite web |title=Shopify jumps more than 14% on news of Nasdaq-100 inclusion |url=https://finance.yahoo.com/news/shopify-stock-jumps-more-14-170308930.html |website=Yahoo! Finance |access-date=12 May 2025}}</ref>

On December 1, 2025, Shopify ended the trading day down 5.9% as some business owners were unable to log into their administrative portals and process retail sales on Shopify's [[Point-of-sale terminal|point of sale]].<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://www.nbcnews.com/business/business-news/shopify-cyber-monday-outage-rcna246818|title=Shopify says a daylong Cyber Monday outage has been resolved|date=2 December 2025|website=NBC News|last=Kopack|first=Steve}}</ref>

In January 2026, Shopify and [[Google]] announced the Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP), an open standard enabling [[Intelligent agent|AI agents]] to discover products and complete transactions with merchants.<ref>{{cite web |last=Mehta |first=Ivan |title=Google announces a new protocol to facilitate commerce using AI agents |url=https://techcrunch.com/2026/01/11/google-announces-a-new-protocol-to-facilitate-commerce-using-ai-agents/ |website=[[TechCrunch]] |date=January 11, 2026 |access-date=March 2, 2026}}</ref><ref>{{cite web |title=Google launches Universal Commerce Protocol, bets on AI-powered retail |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2026/01/11/google-launches-universal-commerce-protocol-bets-on-ai-powered-retail.html |website=[[CNBC]] |date=January 11, 2026 |access-date=March 2, 2026}}</ref>

In May 2026, both [[Cathie Wood]] and [[Thrive Capital]] announced nine figure investments into Shopify.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Thrive Capital Invests $100 Million in Shopify Shares |url=https://www.theinformation.com/briefings/thrive-capital-invests-100-million-shopify-shares |access-date=2026-05-19 |website=The Information |language=en}}</ref>

==== Position on U.S.-Canada tariffs and Canadian retaliation ====
In 2025, Shopify founder and CEO [[Tobias Lütke]] criticized the federal Canadian government for its decision to impose retaliatory tariffs on the United States after President [[Donald Trump]] enacted [[2025 United States trade war with Canada and Mexico|25% tariffs on Canadian goods]].<ref name=":8">{{Cite news |date=2025-02-02 |title=Shopify CEO Lütke says Canada's tariff response 'the wrong choice' |url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/business/article-shopify-ceo-lutke-says-canadas-tariff-response-the-wrong-choice/?login=true |work=The Globe and Mail |language=en-CA}}</ref> Lütke expressed disappointment with Trump's tariffs decision, but added, "Trump believes that Canada has not held its side of the bargain, and he set terms to prove that we still work together: get the borders under control and crack down on fentanyl dens."<ref name=":8" /> Shopify chief operating officer Kaz Nejatian similarly criticized Canadian policies, saying "Canada has turned a blind eye to being used as a training ground for foreign countries, gangs and terrorist groups."<ref name=":8" />

In February 2026, Shopify reported Q4 2025 revenue of $3.67 billion, a 31% year-over-year increase, and full-year 2025 revenue of $11.56 billion, up 30% from 2024, driven by growth in [[business-to-business|B2B]] sales and expanded AI commerce tools. The company also announced a $2 billion [[share buyback]] program.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify's Standout 2025: The Launchpad for a New Era of Commerce in 2026 |url=https://www.shopify.com/news/shopify-q4-2025-financial-results |website=Shopify |access-date=2026-03-21}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |last=Palmer |first=Annie |title=Shopify stock drops despite revenue beat,  billion buyback |url=https://www.cnbc.com/2026/02/11/shopify-shop-earnings-q4-2025.html |date=February 11, 2026 |website=CNBC |access-date=2026-03-21}}</ref>

== Technology ==

Shopify was initially built on Ruby on Rails in 2004, using a single MySQL instance.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Team |first=BlackFlow Technical |date=2025-09-26 |title=How Shopify Scales to 600,000+ Merchants: The Ruby on Rails Architecture That Defied Expectations » BlackFlow |url=https://blackflow.co.uk/entreprise-software-development/how-shopify-scales-to-600000-merchants-the-ruby-on-rails-architecture-that-defied-expectations/ |access-date=2026-04-27 |website=BlackFlow |language=en-US}}</ref> In 2014, Shopify introduced sharding to distribute Shopify to multiple databases. Over the years, Shopify later moved to fully isolated instances.<ref>{{Cite news |url=https://shopify.engineering/e-commerce-at-scale-inside-shopifys-tech-stack |title=E-Commerce at Scale: Inside Shopify's Tech Stack – Stackshare.io |newspaper=Shopify |access-date=March 6, 2023 |archive-date=March 6, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230306000837/https://shopify.engineering/e-commerce-at-scale-inside-shopifys-tech-stack |url-status=live }}</ref>

Shopify maintains Hydrogen, an open-source headless [[JavaScript]] stack created in 2021, for its client-facing storefront applications.<ref>{{cite web |title=Hydrogen |url=https://shopify.dev/docs/api/hydrogen |website=Shopify |access-date=11 April 2024 |language=EN}}</ref> Developers are able to deploy their Hydrogen applications to Oxygen, Shopify's managed hosting and [[content delivery network]]. Hydrogen is built on top of the [[React (software)|React]] library for client-side JavaScript, and [[Remix (web framework)|Remix]] for its server-side routing capabilities.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Jackson |first1=Michael |title=Remixing Shopify |url=https://remix.run/blog/remixing-shopify |website=remix.run |access-date=15 January 2025 |language=en |date=31 October 2022}}</ref>

In 2025, Shopify announced that over the last five years it had migrated all of its apps to [[React Native]] so that the same code could be employed for all client platforms.<ref>{{cite web |last1=Ali |first1=Mustafa |title=Five years of React Native at Shopify |url=https://shopify.engineering/five-years-of-react-native-at-shopify |website=Shopify |access-date=15 January 2025 |language=en |date=13 Jan 2025}}</ref> In their 2026 earnings call Shopify mentioned that their new AI search features is responsible for improving shopping conversion and boosting sales.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Perez |first=Sarah |date=2026-08-05 |title=Shopify says AI search is driving more traffic and sales, not replacing Google |url=https://techcrunch.com/2026/08/05/shopify-says-ai-search-is-driving-more-traffic-and-sales-not-replacing-google/ |access-date=2026-08-09 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref>

== Shopify App Store ==

Shopify launched an [[app store]] on June 2, 2009.<ref>{{Cite web |title=The Story of the New Shopify App Store |url=https://www.shopify.com/partners/blog/story-of-the-new-app-store |access-date=2024-06-06 |website=Shopify |language=en}}</ref> By 2024, the app store had over 10,000 apps available.<ref name=":82">{{Cite web |last=Stone |first=Madeline |title=The Shopify app store is becoming more competitive amid rising costs: 'People are fighting it out on the margins' |url=https://www.businessinsider.com/shopify-app-store-more-competitive-rising-costs-pressures-developers-2024-2 |access-date=2024-06-06 |website=Business Insider |language=en-US}}</ref> As of 2021, a typical Shopify merchant used six apps to manage their business and, in 2020, Shopify app partners collectively earned over $230 million on the platform.<ref name=":92">{{Cite web |date=2021-03-10 |title='Developers have what they need': How Shopify's app ecosystem boosted its core business |url=https://digiday.com/marketing/how-shopifys-app-ecosystem-boosted-its-core-business/ |access-date=2024-06-10 |website=Digiday |language=en-US}}</ref>

In 2021, Shopify announced that it would cut its commission on the first million dollars earned by developers in its app store to 0% following similar moves by [[App Store (Apple)|Apple]], [[Google]], and [[Amazon (company)|Amazon]].<ref>{{Cite web |last=Perez |first=Sarah |date=2021-06-29 |title=Shopify drops its App Store commissions to 0% on developers' first million in revenue |url=https://techcrunch.com/2021/06/29/shopify-drops-its-app-store-commissions-to-0-on-developers-first-million-in-revenue/ |access-date=2024-06-06 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref> In 2022, Shopify partnered with [[Twitter]] to allow merchants to sell products via those social media apps.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Perez |first=Sarah |date=2022-06-22 |title=Twitter partners with Shopify to bring merchants' products to Twitter Shopping |url=https://techcrunch.com/2022/06/22/twitter-partners-with-shopify-to-bring-merchants-products-to-twitter-shopping/ |access-date=2024-06-06 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref> This followed a similar offering with [[TikTok]] in 2020.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Perez |first=Sarah |date=2020-10-27 |title=TikTok partners with Shopify on social commerce |url=https://techcrunch.com/2020/10/27/tiktok-invests-in-social-commerce-via-new-shopify-partnership/ |access-date=2024-06-06 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref> TikTok discontinued this service in 2023.<ref>{{Cite web |title=TikTok forces creators to use TikTok Shop by removing all competition on the app |url=https://www.zdnet.com/article/tiktok-forces-creators-to-use-tiktok-shop-by-removing-all-competition-on-the-app/ |access-date=2024-06-06 |website=ZDNET |language=en}}</ref>

In 2016, Shopify acquired Kit CRM, an app designed to help merchants manage their stores.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify acquires Kit CRM to bolster bot-based messaging for merchants |url=https://www.zdnet.com/article/shopify-acquires-kit-crm-to-bolster-bot-based-messaging-for-merchants/ |access-date=2024-06-07 |website=ZDNET |language=en}}</ref> [[Klaviyo]], the public company providing a marketing automation platform, was originally launched on the Shopify app store in 2012 and received a $100 million strategic investment from Shopify in 2022.<ref name=":82" /><ref>{{Cite web |last=Wiggers |first=Kyle |date=2022-08-02 |title=Shopify makes $100M strategic investment in marketing automation startup Klaviyo |url=https://techcrunch.com/2022/08/02/shopify-makes-100m-strategic-investment-in-marketing-automation-startup-klaviyo/ |access-date=2024-06-11 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref>

== Universal Commerce Protocol and AI commerce ==
In January 2026, Shopify announced an AI project to co-develop what it calls a "Universal Commerce Protocol" (UCP), an open standard designed to allow artificial intelligence agents to connect and transact with merchants across different platforms and user interfaces. UCP was developed in collaboration with Google with the intention of standardizing agentic commerce.<ref>{{cite news |title=Google, Shopify and retailers push AI shopping standard |url=https://www.axios.com/2026/01/11/google-shopify-ai-shopping-standard-nrf-2026 |work=Axios |date=2026-01-11 |access-date=2026-01-16}}</ref>

On 8 September 2026, Shopify announced the addition of Meta to its Agentic Storefronts channels.<ref>{{cite web |title=Meta is now an AI channel in your admin |url=https://changelog.shopify.com/posts/meta-is-now-an-ai-channel-in-your-admin |website=Shopify Changelog |date=2026-09-08}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |last=Albu |first=Sinziana |title=Meta launches personal AI agent with Stripe-powered checkout |url=https://thepaypers.com/payments/news/meta-launches-personal-ai-agent-with-stripe-powered-checkout |work=The Paypers |date=2026-09-09}}</ref>

== Shopify Partner Directory ==
The Shopify Partner Directory<ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify Partner Directory |url=https://www.shopify.com/partners/directory |website=Shopify}}</ref> is a public listing of third-party experts, [[Advertising agency|agencies]], and [[Freelancer|freelancers]] certified by Shopify to assist merchants in building, customizing, or managing their online stores.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2025-01-27 |title=Guide to the Shopify Partner Directory |url=https://stackinfluence.com/guide-to-the-shopify-partner-directory/ |access-date=2025-02-08 |website=stackinfluence.com |language=en-US}}</ref> Launched as part of the Shopify Partners program, the directory includes developers, designers, marketers, and consultants who meet Shopify's eligibility criteria, such as completing training or demonstrating expertise. Merchants can filter partners by services, location, or client reviews.

== Shop Pay ==
{{main|Shop Pay}}

Shop Pay (formerly Shopify Pay) is a checkout and payment method developed by Shopify. Users add shipping and billing information to a Shop account, which enables one-click checkout on online stores that offer Shop Pay.<ref>{{cite web | url=https://techcrunch.com/2017/04/20/shopify-pay-aims-to-complete-the-picture-for-online-checkout/ | title=Shopify Pay aims to complete the picture for online checkout | date=April 20, 2017 }}</ref> Launched in April 2017, it was rebranded as Shop Pay in 2020 and later became an accepted payment method on Facebook and Instagram.<ref>{{Cite web | title=Meer informatie over Shop Pay {{!}} Facebook-helpcentrum | url=https://www.facebook.com/help/273593321031874 | access-date=2025-05-19 | website=www.facebook.com}}</ref> In 2024, Shopify reported that Shop Pay had over 150 million users worldwide.<ref>{{cite web | url=https://www.pymnts.com/earnings/2024/shopifys-merchant-solutions-revenues-up-21percent-underpinned-by-shopify-payments/ | title=Shopify's Merchant Solutions Revenues up 21%, Underpinned by Shopify Payments | date=February 13, 2024 }}</ref>

== Esports ==
{{main|Shopify Rebellion}}

In February 2021, Shopify announced that the company has formed an esports organization called Shopify Rebellion, and put together a professional ''[[StarCraft II]]'' team to compete in international tournaments.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Waters |first=Michael |date=2021-03-02 |title=Why Shopify is investing in esports |url=https://www.modernretail.co/retailers/why-shopify-is-investing-in-esports/ |access-date=2024-10-08 |website=Modern Retail |language=en}}</ref> The team members include former 2016 world champion "ByuN" (Byun Hyun-woo) as well as "[[Scarlett (gamer)|Scarlett]]" (Sasha Hostyn).<ref>{{Cite web|title=The 'StarCraft II' nerds at Shopify now have an esports team|url=https://finance.yahoo.com/news/shopify-esports-team-rebellion-starcraft-ii-200229373.html|access-date=2021-02-20|website=finance.yahoo.com|date=February 19, 2021 |language=en-US|archive-date=February 20, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210220083900/https://finance.yahoo.com/news/shopify-esports-team-rebellion-starcraft-ii-200229373.html|url-status=live}}</ref><ref>{{Cite web |url=https://estnn.com/starcraft-shopify-enters-esports-with-shopify-rebellion/|title=StarCraft: Shopify Enters Esports with Shopify Rebellion | date=February 20, 2021 |access-date=March 2, 2021 |archive-date=February 20, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210220145135/https://estnn.com/starcraft-shopify-enters-esports-with-shopify-rebellion/ |url-status=live}}</ref>

In September 2023, Shopify Rebellion announced it had purchased [[TSM League of Legends division|Team SoloMid]]'s spot in the [[League Championship Series (esports)|LCS]], the main North American [[League of Legends]] esports competition.<ref>
{{cite web |url=https://www.theverge.com/2023/9/20/23883238/league-of-legends-lcs-tsm-replaced-shopify-rebellion |title=Iconic League of Legends team TSM replaced by Shopify in pro league |first=Jay |last=Peters |date=September 20, 2023 |access-date=September 21, 2023 }}</ref>

== Last-mile logistics ==
In April 2021, Shopify made its first entry in last-mile logistics by investing in Swyft, a Toronto-based digital logistics startup.<ref>{{Cite web|date=2021-04-07|title=Inovia, Shopify back delivery startup Swyft in $22 million Series A round {{!}} BetaKit|url=https://betakit.com/inovia-shopify-back-delivery-startup-swyft-in-22-million-series-a-round/|access-date=2021-04-08|language=en-CA|archive-date=April 8, 2021|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210408121553/https://betakit.com/inovia-shopify-back-delivery-startup-swyft-in-22-million-series-a-round/|url-status=live}}</ref> As part of a Series A round of funding, a total of $17.5&nbsp;million was raised for Swyft, co-led by Inovia Capital and Forerunner Ventures with participation from Shopify.<ref>{{Cite web|title=Swyft raises $17.5 million to bring same-day delivery to all the retailers that aren't Amazon|url=https://techcrunch.com/2021/04/07/swyft-raises-17-5-million-to-bring-same-day-delivery-to-all-the-retailers-that-arent-amazon/|access-date=2021-04-08|website=TechCrunch|date=April 7, 2021 |language=en-US|archive-date=August 19, 2022|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220819061036/https://techcrunch.com/2021/04/07/swyft-raises-17-5-million-to-bring-same-day-delivery-to-all-the-retailers-that-arent-amazon/|url-status=live}}</ref>

On May 5, 2022, Shopify announced its acquisition of [[Deliverr]], a [[San Francisco]], California-based ecommerce fulfilment startup, for US$2.1 billion in cash and stock.<ref>{{Cite web |date=May 5, 2022 |title=Shopify acquires shipping logistics startup Deliverr for $2.1B |url=https://techcrunch.com/2022/05/05/shopify-acquires-shipping-logistics-startup-deliverr-for-2-1b/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220819061034/https://techcrunch.com/2022/05/05/shopify-acquires-shipping-logistics-startup-deliverr-for-2-1b/ |archive-date=August 19, 2022 |access-date=2022-05-05 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Durrani |first=Temur |date=2022-05-05 |title=Shopify shares drop on earnings forecast; acquisition of Deliverr set for US$2.1-billion |url=https://www.theglobeandmail.com/business/article-shopify-to-buy-us-based-startup-deliverr-for-us21-billion-shares-fall/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220505203800/https://www.theglobeandmail.com/business/article-shopify-to-buy-us-based-startup-deliverr-for-us21-billion-shares-fall/ |archive-date=May 5, 2022 |access-date=2022-05-05 |work=The Globe and Mail |language=en-CA}}</ref> In May 2023, Shopify wound down its logistics business, selling off its prior related acquisitions; Deliverr and 6 River Systems to [[Flexport]] and [[Ocado Group]] respectively.<ref>{{Cite news |date=2023-05-04 |title=Shopify sells shipping service firm Deliverr, other assets amid narrowing ambitions |work=The Economic Times |url=https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/tech/technology/shopify-sells-shipping-service-firm-deliverr-other-assets-amid-narrowing-ambitions/articleshow/99990262.cms |access-date=2023-05-04 |issn=0013-0389 |archive-date=May 4, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230504182416/https://economictimes.indiatimes.com/tech/technology/shopify-sells-shipping-service-firm-deliverr-other-assets-amid-narrowing-ambitions/articleshow/99990262.cms |url-status=live }}</ref> As part of the Flexport deal, Shopify received a 13% stake in it, besides making Flexport its official logistics partner.<ref name="ap-logistics">{{cite news |last=Hadero |first=Haleluya |date=2023-05-04 |title=Shopify narrowing its ambition, sells Deliverr, cuts staff |url=https://apnews.com/article/shopify-fulfillment-flexport-ocado-group-6b504d0d67acd2ad1af6348cd1ec6425 |work=Associated Press |access-date=May 5, 2023 |archive-date=May 11, 2023 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20230511203234/https://apnews.com/article/shopify-fulfillment-flexport-ocado-group-6b504d0d67acd2ad1af6348cd1ec6425 |url-status=live }}</ref>

== Acquisitions ==
In February 2012, Shopify acquired Select Start Studios Inc ("S3"), a mobile software developer, along with 20 of the company's mobile engineers and designers.<ref>{{cite web |author=Rao, Leena |date=February 1, 2012 |title=E-commerce Platform Shopify Acquires Mobile App Development Studio Select Start |url=https://techcrunch.com/2012/02/01/ecommerce-platform-shopify-acquires-mobile-app-development-studio-select-start/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150218011923/http://techcrunch.com/2012/02/01/ecommerce-platform-shopify-acquires-mobile-app-development-studio-select-start/ |archive-date=February 18, 2015 |access-date=March 4, 2015 |publisher=TechCrunch}}</ref><ref>{{cite news |author=Berkow, Jameson |date=February 1, 2012 |title=Ottawa tech merger: Shopify buys Select Start Studios |url=http://business.financialpost.com/2012/02/01/ottawa-tech-merger-shopify-buys-select-start-studios/?__lsa=556e-fa8c |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200620142344/https://business.financialpost.com/entrepreneur/ottawa-tech-merger-shopify-buys-select-start-studios |archive-date=June 20, 2020 |access-date=March 4, 2015 |newspaper=Financial Post}}</ref> In August 2013, Shopify acquired Jet Cooper, a 25-person design studio based in Toronto.<ref>{{cite web |author=Lindzon, Jared |date=August 1, 2013 |title=Shopify acquires design agency Jet Cooper |url=http://www.betakit.com/shopify-acquires-design-agency-jet-cooper/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141202205520/http://www.betakit.com/shopify-acquires-design-agency-jet-cooper/ |archive-date=December 2, 2014 |access-date=March 4, 2015 |publisher=BetaKit}}</ref>

On December 5, 2016, Shopify acquired Toronto-based mobile product development studio Tiny Hearts.<ref>{{Cite web |title=Shopify acquires mobile app design house Tiny Hearts {{!}} Retail Dive |url=https://www.retaildive.com/news/shopify-acquires-mobile-app-design-house-tiny-hearts/431710/ |access-date=2026-04-27 |website=www.retaildive.com |language=en-US}}</ref> The Tiny Hearts building has been turned into a Shopify research and development office.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Perez |first=Sarah |date=2016-12-05 |title=Shopify acquires digital studio Tiny Hearts, makers of Wake Alarm, Next Keyboard & more |url=https://techcrunch.com/2016/12/05/shopify-acquires-digital-studio-tiny-hearts-makers-of-wake-alarm-next-keyboard-more/ |access-date=2024-10-08 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref>

In May 2019, Shopify acquired Handshake, a business-to-business e-commerce platform for wholesale goods.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Noto |first=Anthony |title=Shopify buys Handshake for $100M to boost B2B commerce tools |url=https://www.bizjournals.com/newyork/news/2019/05/24/shopify-buys-handshake-to-boost-b2b-commerce-tools.html |access-date=2026-04-27 |website=www.bizjournals.com}}</ref> The Handshake team was integrated into Shopify Plus, and Handshake founder and CEO Glen Coates was made Director of Product for Shopify Plus.<ref>{{Cite web |date=May 23, 2019 |title=Shopify quietly acquired Handshake, an e-commerce platform for B2B wholesale purchasing |url=https://techcrunch.com/2019/05/23/shopify-quietly-acquired-handshake-an-e-commerce-platform-for-b2b-wholesale-purchasing/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220819061034/https://techcrunch.com/2019/05/23/shopify-quietly-acquired-handshake-an-e-commerce-platform-for-b2b-wholesale-purchasing/ |archive-date=August 19, 2022 |access-date=May 29, 2019 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref> In June 2019, Shopify announced that it would launch its Fulfilment Network. The service promises to handle shipping logistics for merchants and will compete with an established leader, Amazon FBA. Shopify Fulfillment Network will be available to qualifying U.S. merchants in select states.<ref>{{Cite news |date=June 19, 2019 |title=Shopify Is Setting Up Fulfillment Network in U.S., Just Like Amazon |url=https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2019-06-19/shopify-setting-up-fulfillment-network-in-u-s-just-like-amazon |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20190801132848/https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2019-06-19/shopify-setting-up-fulfillment-network-in-u-s-just-like-amazon |archive-date=August 1, 2019 |access-date=July 31, 2019 |newspaper=Bloomberg.com}}</ref> On September 9, 2019, Shopify announced the acquisition of 6 River Systems, a Massachusetts-based company that makes warehouse robots. The acquisition was finalized in October, resulting in a cash-and-share deal worth US$450&nbsp;million.<ref>{{Cite web |date=December 16, 2019 |title=Deals of the Year – Tech: Shopify feeling fulfilled with $450M acquisition {{!}} Ottawa Business Journal |url=https://obj.ca/article/deals-year-tech-shopify-feeling-fulfilled-450m-acquisition |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191217063833/https://obj.ca/article/deals-year-tech-shopify-feeling-fulfilled-450m-acquisition |archive-date=December 17, 2019 |access-date=January 9, 2020 |website=obj.ca}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news |last=Innovation |date=January 7, 2020 |title=Shopify puts late 2019 swoon behind it as shares hit new all-time high {{!}} Financial Post |url=https://business.financialpost.com/technology/shopify-puts-late-2019-swoon-behind-it-as-shares-hit-new-all-time-high |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200108162530/https://business.financialpost.com/technology/shopify-puts-late-2019-swoon-behind-it-as-shares-hit-new-all-time-high |archive-date=January 8, 2020 |access-date=January 9, 2020 |newspaper=Financial Post |language=en-CA}}</ref>

On June 11, 2021, Shopify announced its acquisition of Primer, an [[Augmented reality|AR]] app on the [[App Store (iOS/iPadOS)|App Store]] that allows users to preview home improvement items digitally.<ref>{{Cite web |date=June 11, 2021 |title=Shopify brings on team from augmented reality home design app Primer |url=https://techcrunch.com/2021/06/11/shopify-acquires-augmented-reality-home-design-app-primer/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20210629180058/https://techcrunch.com/2021/06/11/shopify-acquires-augmented-reality-home-design-app-primer/ |archive-date=June 29, 2021 |access-date=2021-06-29 |language=en-US}}</ref> On April 11, 2022, Shopify announced its acquisition of Dovetale, an influencer marketing startup from New York.<ref>{{Cite web |date=2022-04-13 |title=Shopify acquires influencer marketing startup Dovetale |url=https://ecommercenews.eu/shopify-acquires-influencer-marketing-startup-dovetale/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20220421222300/https://ecommercenews.eu/shopify-acquires-influencer-marketing-startup-dovetale/ |archive-date=April 21, 2022 |access-date=2022-05-12 |website=Ecommerce News |language=en-US}}</ref> In October, the company acquired [[Remix (web framework)|Remix]], a full-stack [[TypeScript]] framework that provides "snappy page loads and instant transitions".<ref>{{Cite web |last=Wiggers |first=Kyle |date=31 October 2022 |title=Shopify acquires Remix to bolster its storefront design tools |url=https://techcrunch.com/2022/10/31/shopify-acquires-remix-to-bolster-its-storefront-design-tools/|access-date=13 August 2024 |website=TechCrunch}}</ref>

On June 3, 2024, Shopify announced its acquisition of Checkout Blocks, an app on its App Store "that enables merchants to unlock customized extensibility" with no-code customizations in checkout.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Forristal |first=Lauren |date=2024-06-03 |title=Shopify acquires Checkout Blocks, a checkout customization app |url=https://techcrunch.com/2024/06/03/shopify-checkout-blocks-acquisition/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20240707203249/https://techcrunch.com/2024/06/03/shopify-checkout-blocks-acquisition/ |archive-date=July 7, 2024 |access-date=2024-07-07 |website=TechCrunch |language=en-US}}</ref> Also in June 2024, Shopify acquired business communication startup Threads for an undisclosed amount of money.<ref name=techcrunch>{{cite web|url=https://techcrunch.com/2024/06/07/shopify-acquires-threads-no-not-that-one/ |title=Shopify acquires Threads (no, not that one) | first=Ivan|last=Mehta|work=TechCrunch	|date=June 7, 2024|access-date=January 16, 2025}}</ref>

== Recognition ==
Shopify was named Ottawa's Fastest Growing Company by the [[Ottawa Business Journal]] in 2010.<ref name="ottawabusinessjournal">Donnelly, Jim. [https://creativerebel.de/fastest-growing-companies-1-shopify/ Fastest Growing Companies] {{Webarchive|url=https://web.archive.org/web/20191107211924/https://creativerebel.de/fastest-growing-companies-1-shopify/ |date=November 7, 2019 }} ''[[Ottawa Business Journal]]''. May 3, 2010.</ref>

By 2014, the platform had hosted approximately 120,000 online retailers,<ref name=raises7m/><ref name=Redesign/> and was listed as #3 in [[Deloitte]]'s Fast50 in Canada, as well as #7 in Deloitte's Fast 500 of North America.<ref>{{cite web |author=Louis Columbus |date=November 20, 2014 |title=Software Companies Are Dominating Deloitte's Technology Fast 500 |url=https://www.forbes.com/sites/louiscolumbus/2014/11/20/software-companies-are-dominating-deloittes-technology-fast-500/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150204174436/http://www.forbes.com/sites/louiscolumbus/2014/11/20/software-companies-are-dominating-deloittes-technology-fast-500/ |archive-date=February 4, 2015 |access-date=February 5, 2015 |website=[[Forbes]]}}</ref>

== Controversies ==
In 2017, the #DeleteShopify [[Hashtag activism|hashtag campaign]] called for a boycott of Shopify for allowing ''[[Breitbart News]]'' to host a shop on its platform.<ref>{{cite magazine | "refusing to do business with the site would constitute a violation of free speech".<ref>{{cite web| url=https://www.recode.net/2017/2/8/14555636/shopify-not-kicking-breitbart-out| title=Breitbart In October 2017, Citron Research founder, short-seller [[Andrew Left]] released a report that claimed Shopify was overstating the number of merchants using the e-commerce platform and described it as a "get-rich-quick" scheme in contravention of [[Federal Trade Commission]] regulations.<ref name=":1">{{Cite news|url=http://www.bnn.ca/shopify-s-ceo-calls-out-citron-s-andrew-left-as-short-selling-troll-1.881400|title=Shopify's CEO calls out Citron's Andrew Left as 'short-selling troll' – Article – Herbalife'|work=Business Insider|access-date=November 27, 2017|language=en|archive-date=November 9, 2017|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20171109225205/http://www.businessinsider.com/shopify-citron-andrew-left-business-dirtier-than-herbalife-2017-10|url-status=live}}</ref> The day the report was released, the stock plunged more than 11%.<ref name=":3">{{Cite news|url=https://finance.yahoo.com/news/ignore-citron-report-dip-buy-090032601.html|title=Ignore Citron Report and Use This Dip to Buy Shopify Inc (US) (SHOP) Stock|access-date=November 27, 2017|language=en-US|archive-date=September 20, 2018|archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180920121851/https://finance.yahoo.com/news/ignore-citron-report-dip-buy-090032601.html|url-status=live}}</ref> Left wrote another report about Shopify in April 2019, stating he believed Shopify's stock price would come down 50% in the next 12 months.<ref>{{Cite web|url=https://realmoney.thestreet.com/investing/stocks/shopify-shares-fall-fast-following-report-from-short-seller-14917578|title=Shopify Shares Fall Fast Following Report From Short Seller|last=Curran|first=Kevin|date=April 4, 2019|website=RealMoney|language=en|access-date=October 30, 2019|archive-date=October 26, In January 2020, Left announced in his annual letter to investors that Citron Research had exited the short position. The reports did not lead to an investigation into Shopify by the FTC.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Litalien |first=Mat |date=2020-01-11 |title=Shopify's (SHOP) Stock Price Soars As Short Seller Throws in the Towel |url=https://www.fool.ca/2020/01/11/shopifys-shop-stock-price-soars-as-short-seller-throws-in-the-towel/ |url-status=live |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20200111170144/https://www.fool.ca/2020/01/11/shopifys-shop-stock-price-soars-as-short-seller-throws-in-the-towel/ |archive-date=2020-01-11 |access-date=2023-03-12 |website=The Motley Fool Canada |language=en-CA}}</ref>

In October 2018, an article in [[The Logic]] found several groups identified by [[Southern Poverty Law Center]] as "hate groups" were using Shopify's platform for their online stores.<ref>{{Cite web |last=Craig |first=Sean |date=2018-10-11 |title=E-commerce companies are still processing payment transactions for extremists |url=https://thelogic.co/news/retail/e-commerce-companies-are-still-processing-payment-transactions-for-extremists/ |access-date=2025-02-11 |website=The Logic |language=en-US}}</ref><ref name=":9">{{Cite web |author1=James Temperton |author2=Murad Hemmadi |date=2025-02-10 |title=Shopify tells support staff to give 'no comment' on Kanye West Nazi T-shirt |url=https://thelogic.co/news/exclusive/kanye-west-yeezy-shopify-swastika-shirt/ |access-date=2025-02-11 |website=The Logic |language=en-US}}</ref> 

In July 2022, Shopify was criticized by left-leaning media watchdog [[Media Matters for America]] for hosting the online store for [[Libs of TikTok]].<ref name=":6" /><ref name=":7">{{Cite web |last=Carter |first=Camden |title=Shopify is allowing anti-LGBTQ "Libs of TikTok" to sell merchandise on its platform |language=en |archive-date=February 23, 2023
